\displaystyle+Q_{\sigma}(0<g(\frac{\|X-x_{m}\|}{r})\leq(\frac{t}{C_{\beta}r^{\beta}})^{\frac{1}{\beta}})

\displaystyle=((m-1)w+w_{0})\mathbf{1}\{t\geq C_{\beta}r^{\beta}\}

\displaystyle\leq mw\mathbf{1}\{t\geq C_{\beta}r^{\beta}\}

\displaystyle\leq c^{\prime}_{m}c_{w}r^{\alpha\beta}\mathbf{1}\{t\geq C_{\beta}r^{\beta}\}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\alpha}t^{\alpha}.

given that
c_{m}
is small enough since
c_{m}^{\prime}\overset{c_{m}\rightarrow 0}{\longrightarrow 0}
. Therefore, it holds that
Q_{\sigma}\in\mathcal{M}(\alpha,C_{\alpha})
.

Verify Smoothness Assumption:

It is easy to see that for any
a,b\in[0,2]
we have

|g^{\beta}(a)-g^{\beta}(b)|\leq|a-b|^{\beta}.

Thus, for any
x,x^{\prime}\in B(x_{k},2r)
, we obtain from the triangular inequality
\|x-x_{k}\|-\|x^{\prime}-x_{k}\|\leq\|x-x^{\prime}\|
that

|r^{\beta}g^{\beta}(\|x-x_{k}\|/r))-r^{\beta}g^{\beta}(\|x^{\prime}-x_{k}\|/r))|\leq\|x-x^{\prime}\|^{\beta},

(63)

Therefore, by the definition of
\eta^{Q}_{\sigma}
and (
63
), we see that

|\eta^{Q}_{\sigma}(x)-\eta^{Q}_{\sigma}(x^{\prime})|\leq C_{\beta}\|x-x^{\prime}\|^{\beta},\quad\forall x,x\in[0,1]^{d}.

\eta^{P}
is smooth with any smoothness parameter
\beta_{P}
.

Verify Strong Density Condition:

If
x\in B(x_{k},r)
for some
k=1,\cdots,m-1
, we have

\mu(x)=\frac{w}{\lambda[B(x_{k},r)]}=c_{w}/\pi_{d}.

If
x\in B(x_{m},r_{0})
we have

\mu(x)=\frac{w_{0}}{\lambda[B(x_{m},r_{0})]}=c_{w}/\pi_{d}.

If
x\in B_{c}
, we have

(m-1)w+w_{0}\leq mw\leq c^{\prime}_{m}r^{\alpha\beta}\overset{n_{Q}\rightarrow\infty}{\longrightarrow}0,

and

m\lambda[B(x_{k},2r)]\leq c^{\prime}_{m}2^{d}\pi_{d}r^{\alpha\beta}\overset{n_{Q}\rightarrow\infty}{\longrightarrow}0.

Hence,

\mu(x)=\frac{1-(m-1)w-w_{0}}{1-m\lambda[B(x_{k},2r)]}\overset{n_{Q}\rightarrow\infty}{\longrightarrow}1.

Combining all cases above, we could set
c_{w}\in[\pi_{d}\mu^{-},\pi_{d}\mu^{+}]
to satisfy the condition
(Q_{\sigma},P)\in\mathcal{S}(\mu)
.

Verify Ambiguity Level:

Since
\Omega=\Omega_{P}
, we trivially have that

\Omega^{-}(\gamma,C_{\gamma})\subset\{x\in\Omega:\eta^{Q}(x)\neq\frac{1}{2}\}=:\Omega^{-}.

Then,

\displaystyle A(z):=

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega^{-}(\gamma,C_{\gamma})}|\eta^{Q}(X)-\frac{1}{2}|\mathbf{1}\{0<|\eta^{Q}(X)-\frac{1}{2}|\leq z\}dQ_{X}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega^{-}}|\eta^{Q}(X)-\frac{1}{2}|\mathbf{1}\{0<|\eta^{Q}(X)-\frac{1}{2}|\leq z\}dQ_{X}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle((m-1)w+w_{0})C_{\beta}r^{\beta}\mathbf{1}\{z\geq C_{\beta}r^{\beta}\}.

If
z<C_{\beta}r^{\beta}
, we have

A(z)=0\leq\varepsilon(z;\gamma,C_{\gamma}).

If
z\geq C_{\beta}r^{\beta}
, we have

A(z)\leq C_{\beta}mwr^{\beta}\leq 2c_{m}\varepsilon(C_{\beta}c_{r}^{\beta}n_{Q}^{-\frac{\beta}{2\beta+d}};\gamma,C_{\gamma})\leq\varepsilon(z;\gamma,C_{\gamma}).

Therefore, the given ambiguity level
\varepsilon(z;\gamma,C_{\gamma})
is well-defined provided that
c_{m}\leq\frac{1}{2}
.

Putting all verification steps above together, we conclude that
(Q_{\sigma},P)\in\Pi_{S}^{NP}
. We finish the proof of this part by applying Assouad’s lemma to
(Q_{\sigma},P),\ \forall\sigma\in\{1,-1\}^{m}
.

Suppose that
\sigma,\sigma^{\prime}\in\{1,-1\}^{m}
differ only at one coordinate, i.e.

\sigma_{k}=-\sigma^{\prime}_{k},\quad\sigma_{l}=\sigma^{\prime}_{l}\ (\forall l\neq k).

If
k\neq m
, we have the Hellinger distance bound as

\displaystyle H^{2}(Q_{\sigma},Q_{\sigma^{\prime}})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\int\left(\sqrt{\eta^{Q}_{\sigma}(X)}-\sqrt{\eta^{Q}_{\sigma^{\prime}}(X)}\right)^{2}+\left(\sqrt{1-\eta^{Q}_{\sigma}(X)}-\sqrt{1-\eta^{Q}_{\sigma^{\prime}}(X)}\right)^{2}dQ_{X}

\displaystyle=\int_{B(x_{k},r)}\frac{w}{\lambda[B(x_{k},r)]}\left(\sqrt{\frac{1}{2}+C_{\beta}r^{\beta}}-\sqrt{\frac{1}{2}-C_{\beta}r^{\beta}}\right)^{2}dx

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}w(1-\sqrt{1-2C^{2}_{\beta}r^{2\beta}})

\displaystyle\leq C^{2}_{\beta}wr^{2\beta}.

Similarly, if
k=m
, the Hellinger distance bound should be

H^{2}(Q_{\sigma},Q_{\sigma^{\prime}})\leq C^{2}_{\beta}w_{0}r^{2\beta}\leq C^{2}_{\beta}wr^{2\beta}.

Recall that
r=c_{r}n_{Q}^{-\frac{1}{2\beta+d}}
. By the property of Hellinger distance, we have

H^{2}(\mathbb{P}^{\sigma}_{\mathcal{D}_{Q}},\mathbb{P}^{\sigma^{\prime}}_{\mathcal{D}_{Q}})\leq n_{Q}H^{2}(Q_{\sigma},Q_{\sigma^{\prime}})\leq n_{Q}C^{2}_{\beta}wr^{2\beta}\leq C^{2}_{\beta}c_{r}^{2\beta+d}c_{w}\leq\frac{\sqrt{2}}{4}

provided that
c_{r}
is small enough. This further indicates that

TV(\mathbb{P}^{\sigma}_{\mathcal{D}_{Q}},\mathbb{P}^{\sigma^{\prime}}_{\mathcal{D}_{Q}})\leq\sqrt{2}H^{2}(\mathbb{P}^{\sigma}_{\mathcal{D}_{Q}},\mathbb{P}^{\sigma^{\prime}}_{\mathcal{D}_{Q}})\leq\frac{1}{2}.

(64)

For any empirical classifier
\hat{f}
, if
k\neq m
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{Q_{\sigma}}(\hat{f})+\mathcal{E}_{Q_{\sigma^{\prime}}}(\hat{f})

\displaystyle=2\mathbb{E}_{Q_{\sigma}}[|\eta^{Q_{\sigma}}(X)-\frac{1}{2}|\mathbf{1}\{\hat{f}(X)\neq f^{*}_{Q_{\sigma}}(X)\}]

\displaystyle+2\mathbb{E}_{Q_{\sigma^{\prime}}}[|\eta^{Q_{\sigma^{\prime}}}(X)-\frac{1}{2}|\mathbf{1}\{\hat{f}(X)\neq f^{*}_{Q_{\sigma^{\prime}}}(X)\}]

\displaystyle\geq 2\int_{B(x_{k},r)}\frac{w}{\lambda[B(x_{k},r)]}C_{\beta}r^{\beta}(\mathbf{1}\{\hat{f}(X)\neq f^{*}_{Q_{\sigma}}(X)\}+\mathbf{1}\{\hat{f}(X)\neq f^{*}_{Q_{\sigma^{\prime}}}(X)\})dx

\displaystyle=2C_{\beta}wr^{\beta}.

If
k=m
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{Q_{\sigma}}(\hat{f})+\mathcal{E}_{Q_{\sigma^{\prime}}}(\hat{f})

\displaystyle=2\mathbb{E}_{Q_{\sigma}}[|\eta^{Q_{\sigma}}(X)-\frac{1}{2}|\mathbf{1}\{\hat{f}(X)\neq f^{*}_{Q_{\sigma}}(X)\}]

\displaystyle+2\mathbb{E}_{Q_{\sigma^{\prime}}}[|\eta^{Q_{\sigma^{\prime}}}(X)-\frac{1}{2}|\mathbf{1}\{\hat{f}(X)\neq f^{*}_{Q_{\sigma^{\prime}}}(X)\}]

\displaystyle\geq 2\int_{B(x_{m},r_{0})}\frac{w_{0}}{\lambda[B(x_{m},r_{0})]}C_{\beta}r^{\beta}(\mathbf{1}\{\hat{f}(X)\neq f^{*}_{Q_{\sigma}}(X)\}+\mathbf{1}\{\hat{f}(X)\neq f^{*}_{Q_{\sigma^{\prime}}}(X)\})dx

\displaystyle=2C_{\beta}w_{0}r^{\beta}.

Combining this lower bound with (
64
), the Assouad’s lemma shows that

\sup_{(Q,P)\in\Pi_{S}^{NP}}\mathbb{E}\mathcal{E}_{Q}(\hat{f})\geq\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}(Q_{\sigma},P)\\
\sigma\in\{1,-1\}^{m}\end{subarray}}\mathbb{E}\mathcal{E}_{Q_{\sigma}}(\hat{f})\geq\frac{1}{2}C_{\beta}((m-1)w+w_{0})r^{\beta}.

On one hand, if
m\geq 2
, we further have

\sup_{(Q,P)\in\Pi_{S}^{NP}}\mathbb{E}\mathcal{E}_{Q}(\hat{f})\geq\frac{1}{2}C_{\beta}((m-1)w+w_{0})r^{\beta}\geq\frac{1}{4}C_{\beta}mwr^{\beta}\gtrsim\varepsilon(C_{\beta}c_{r}^{\beta}n_{Q}^{-\frac{\beta}{2\beta+d}};\gamma,C_{\gamma}).

On the other hand, if
m=1
, we have

r_{0}=\left(\frac{c_{m}\varepsilon(C_{\beta}c_{r}^{\beta}n_{Q}^{-\frac{\beta}{2\beta+d}};\gamma,C_{\gamma})}{C_{\beta}c_{w}}\right)^{\frac{1}{\beta+d}},\quad w_{0}=c_{w}r_{0}^{d}.

Therefore,

\sup_{(Q,P)\in\Pi_{S}^{NP}}\mathbb{E}\mathcal{E}_{Q}(\hat{f})\geq\frac{1}{2}C_{\beta}w_{0}r^{\beta}\geq\frac{1}{2}C_{\beta}c_{w}r_{0}^{\beta+d}\gtrsim\varepsilon(C_{\beta}c_{r}^{\beta}n_{Q}^{-\frac{\beta}{2\beta+d}};\gamma,C_{\gamma}).

We conclude that it always holds that
\sup_{(Q,P)\in\Pi_{BA}^{NP}}\mathbb{E}\mathcal{E}_{Q}(\hat{f})\gtrsim\varepsilon(C_{\beta}c_{r}^{\beta}n_{Q}^{-\frac{\beta}{2\beta+d}};\gamma,C_{\gamma})
, which finishes the proof.
∎

Following a similar argument with the ones in the proofs of Theorem
5
(2), (3) and Theorem
11
, it suffices to show that

\inf_{\hat{f}}\sup_{(Q,P)\in\Pi_{BA}^{NP}}\mathbb{E}\mathcal{E}_{Q}(\hat{f})\gtrsim\Delta^{\frac{1+\alpha}{\gamma}}\land n_{Q}^{-\frac{\beta(1+\alpha)}{2\beta+d}}.

(65)

for any setting of
\gamma
and
\beta
.

Fix
\eta^{P}\equiv\frac{1}{2}
. Since
\eta^{P}
is a constant, it is trivial that
\eta^{P}\in\mathcal{H}(\beta_{P},C_{\beta_{P}})
for any
\beta_{P},C_{\beta_{P}}
. Next, define the quantities

r=(c_{r}n_{Q}^{-\frac{1}{2\beta+d}})\land(C_{\beta}^{-\frac{1}{\beta}}(\frac{\Delta}{C_{\gamma}})^{\frac{1}{\gamma\beta}}),\quad w=c_{w}r^{d},\quad m=\lfloor c_{m}r^{\alpha\beta-d}\rfloor,

where the constants
c_{r},c_{w},c_{m}
will be specified later in the proof.

We consider a packing
\{x_{k}\}_{k=1,\cdots,m}
with radius
2r
in
[0,1]^{d}
. We repeat the example of such construction given in Proposition
3
. Divide
[0,1]^{d}
into uniform small cubes with side length as
6r
, which forms a grid with at least
\lfloor(6r)^{-1}\rfloor^{d}
small cubes. Since
m\ll\lfloor(6r)^{-1}\rfloor^{d}
, we suppose that
c_{m}
is small enough such that
m<\lfloor(6r)^{-1}\rfloor^{d}
. Therefore, we could assign exactly one sphere with radius
2r
in one cube without intersection, which forms a packing of
\{x_{k}\}_{k=1,\cdots,m}
with radius
2r
in
[0,1]^{d}
. Also, define
B_{c}
as the compliment of these
m
balls, i.e.

B_{c}:=[0,1]^{d}/\left(\bigcup_{k=1}^{m}B(x_{k},2r)\right)
.

For any
\sigma\in\{1,-1\}^{m}
, we consider the regression function
\eta^{Q}_{\sigma}(x)
defined as follows:

\eta^{Q}_{\sigma}(x)=\begin{cases}\frac{1}{2}+\sigma_{k}C_{\beta}r^{\beta}g^{\beta}(\frac{\|x-x_{k}\|}{r})\quad&\text{if $x\in B(x_{k},2r)$ for some $k=1,\cdots,m$}\\
\frac{1}{2}\quad&\text{otherwise,}\end{cases}

where the function
g(\cdot)
is defined as
g(x)=\min\{1,2-x\}
on
x\in[0,2]
.

The construction of the marginal distributions
Q_{\sigma,X}
is as follows. Let
Q_{\sigma,X}
have the density function
\mu(\cdot)
defined as

\mu(x)=\begin{cases}\frac{w}{\lambda[B(x_{k},r)]}\quad&\text{if $x\in B(x_{k},r)$ for some $k=1,\cdots,m$,}\\
\frac{1-mw}{1-m\lambda[B(x_{k},2r)]}\quad&\text{if $x\in B_{c}$,}\\
0\quad&\text{otherwise.}\end{cases}

Since the density function
\mu(\cdot)
does not depend on the specific choice of
\sigma
, we fix
P_{X}=Q_{\sigma,X}
for any
\sigma\in\{1,-1\}^{m}
. Given the the construction of
Q_{\sigma}
, we next verify that
(Q_{\sigma},P)
belongs to
\Pi_{BA}^{NP}
for any
\sigma\in\{1,-1\}^{m}
.

Verify Margin Assumption:
We have that

\displaystyle Q_{\sigma}(0<|\eta_{\sigma}^{Q}-\frac{1}{2}|<t)

\displaystyle=mQ_{\sigma}(0<C_{\beta}r^{\beta}g^{\beta}(\frac{\|X-x_{1}\|}{r})\leq t)

\displaystyle=mQ_{\sigma}(0<g(\frac{\|X-x_{1}\|}{r})\leq(\frac{t}{C_{\beta}r^{\beta}})^{\frac{1}{\beta}})

\displaystyle=mw\mathbf{1}\{t\geq C_{\beta}r^{\beta}\}

\displaystyle\leq c_{m}c_{w}r^{\alpha\beta}\mathbf{1}\{t\geq C_{\beta}r^{\beta}\}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\alpha}t^{\alpha}.

given that
c_{m}
is small enough. Therefore,
Q_{\sigma}\in\mathcal{M}(\alpha,C_{\alpha})
.

Verify Smoothness Assumption:

It is easy to see that for any
a,b\in[0,2]
we have

|g^{\beta}(a)-g^{\beta}(b)|\leq|a-b|^{\beta}.

Thus, for any
x,x^{\prime}\in B(x_{k},2r)
, we obtain from the triangular inequality
\|x-x_{k}\|-\|x^{\prime}-x_{k}\|\leq\|x-x^{\prime}\|
that

|r^{\beta}g^{\beta}(\|x-x_{k}\|/r))-r^{\beta}g^{\beta}(\|x^{\prime}-x_{k}\|/r))|\leq\|x-x^{\prime}\|^{\beta}.

(66)

Therefore, by the definition of
\eta^{Q}_{\sigma}
and (
66
), we see that

|\eta^{Q}_{\sigma}(x)-\eta^{Q}_{\sigma}(x^{\prime})|\leq C_{\beta}\|x-x^{\prime}\|^{\beta},\quad\forall x,x\in[0,1]^{d}.

Verify Strong Density Condition:

If
x\in B(x_{k},r)
for some
k=1,\cdots,m
, we have

\mu(x)=\frac{w}{\lambda[B(x_{k},r)]}=c_{w}/\pi_{d}.

If
x\in B_{c}
, we have

\mu(x)=\frac{1-c_{w}\lfloor c_{m}r^{\alpha\beta-d}\rfloor r^{d}}{1-2^{d}\pi_{d}\lfloor c_{m}r^{\alpha\beta-d}\rfloor r^{d}}\overset{n_{Q}\rightarrow\infty}{\longrightarrow}1.

Therefore, we could set
c_{w}\in[\pi_{d}\mu^{-},\pi_{d}\mu^{+}]
to satisfy the condition
(Q_{\sigma},P)\in\mathcal{S}(\mu)
.

Verify Band-like Ambiguity:

Since
\eta^{P}\equiv\frac{1}{2}
, by Definition
1
we have
s(x)=0
for any
x\in\Omega
.
In addition, by definition of
\eta^{Q}_{\sigma}
and
r
, we have that for any
x\in\Omega
,

|\eta^{Q}_{\sigma}-\frac{1}{2}|\leq C_{\beta}r^{\beta}\leq(\frac{\Delta}{C_{\gamma}})^{\frac{1}{\gamma}}\Rightarrow C_{\gamma}|\eta^{Q}_{\sigma}-\frac{1}{2}|^{\gamma}\leq\Delta.

Therefore, we have

s(x)=0\geq C_{\gamma}|\eta^{Q}_{\sigma}-\frac{1}{2}|^{\gamma}-\Delta

for any
x\in\Omega
.

Putting all verification steps above together, we conclude that
(Q_{\sigma},P)\in\Pi_{BA}^{NP}
. We finish the proof of this part by applying Assouad’s lemma to
(Q_{\sigma},P),\ \forall\sigma\in\{1,-1\}^{m}
.

If
\sigma,\sigma^{\prime}\in\{1,-1\}^{m}
differ only at one coordinate, i.e.

\sigma_{k}=-\sigma^{\prime}_{k},\quad\sigma_{l}=\sigma^{\prime}_{l}\ (\forall l\neq k),

we have the Hellinger distance bound as

\displaystyle H^{2}(Q_{\sigma},Q_{\sigma^{\prime}})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\int\left(\sqrt{\eta^{Q}_{\sigma}(X)}-\sqrt{\eta^{Q}_{\sigma^{\prime}}(X)}\right)^{2}+\left(\sqrt{1-\eta^{Q}_{\sigma}(X)}-\sqrt{1-\eta^{Q}_{\sigma^{\prime}}(X)}\right)^{2}dQ_{X}

\displaystyle=\int_{B(x_{k},r)}\frac{w}{\lambda[B(x_{k},r)]}\left(\sqrt{\frac{1}{2}+C_{\beta}r^{\beta}}-\sqrt{\frac{1}{2}-C_{\beta}r^{\beta}}\right)^{2}dx

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}w(1-\sqrt{1-2C^{2}_{\beta}r^{2\beta}})

\displaystyle\leq C^{2}_{\beta}wr^{2\beta}.

Recall that
r=(c_{r}n_{Q}^{-\frac{1}{2\beta+d}})\land(C_{\beta}^{-\frac{1}{\beta}}(\frac{\Delta}{C_{\gamma}})^{\frac{1}{\gamma\beta}})
. By the property of Hellinger distance, we have

H^{2}(\mathbb{P}^{\sigma}_{\mathcal{D}_{Q}},\mathbb{P}^{\sigma^{\prime}}_{\mathcal{D}_{Q}})\leq n_{Q}H^{2}(Q_{\sigma},Q_{\sigma^{\prime}})\leq C^{2}_{\beta}wn_{Q}r^{2\beta}\leq C^{2}_{\beta}c_{r}^{2\beta+d}c_{w}\leq\frac{\sqrt{2}}{4}

provided that
c_{r}
is small enough. This further indicates that

TV(\mathbb{P}^{\sigma}_{\mathcal{D}_{Q}},\mathbb{P}^{\sigma^{\prime}}_{\mathcal{D}_{Q}})\leq\sqrt{2}H^{2}(\mathbb{P}^{\sigma}_{\mathcal{D}_{Q}},\mathbb{P}^{\sigma^{\prime}}_{\mathcal{D}_{Q}})\leq\frac{1}{2}.

(67)

For any empirical classifier
\hat{f}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{Q_{\sigma}}(\hat{f})+\mathcal{E}_{Q_{\sigma^{\prime}}}(\hat{f})

\displaystyle=2\mathbb{E}_{Q_{\sigma}}[|\eta^{Q_{\sigma}}(X)-\frac{1}{2}|\mathbf{1}\{\hat{f}(X)\neq f^{*}_{Q_{\sigma}}(X)\}]

\displaystyle+2\mathbb{E}_{Q_{\sigma^{\prime}}}[|\eta^{Q_{\sigma^{\prime}}}(X)-\frac{1}{2}|\mathbf{1}\{\hat{f}(X)\neq f^{*}_{Q_{\sigma^{\prime}}}(X)\}]

\displaystyle\geq 2\int_{B(x_{k},r)}\frac{w}{\lambda[B(x_{k},r)]}C_{\beta}r^{\beta}(\mathbf{1}\{\hat{f}(X)\neq f^{*}_{Q_{\sigma}}(X)\}+\mathbf{1}\{\hat{f}(X)\neq f^{*}_{Q_{\sigma^{\prime}}}(X)\})dx

\displaystyle=2C_{\beta}wr^{\beta}.

Combining this lower bound with (
67
), the Assouad’s lemma shows that

\sup_{(Q,P)\in\Pi_{BA}^{NP}}\mathbb{E}\mathcal{E}_{Q}(\hat{f})\geq\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}(Q_{\sigma},P)\\
\sigma\in\{1,-1\}^{m}\end{subarray}}\mathbb{E}\mathcal{E}_{Q_{\sigma}}(\hat{f})\geq\frac{1}{2}C_{\beta}mwr^{\beta}\gtrsim r^{\beta(1+\alpha)}\gtrsim\Delta^{\frac{1+\alpha}{\gamma}}\land n_{Q}^{-\frac{\beta(1+\alpha)}{2\beta+d}}.

∎

D
Proofs in Parametric Classification

We first list some definitions that are helpful for the proofs in this section. Denote the target and source distribution with certain linear coefficients
\beta,w
as

Q_{\beta}:=\{Q:X\sim N(0,I_{d}),\eta^{Q}(x)=\sigma(\beta^{T}x)\},

P_{w}:=\{Q:X\sim N(0,I_{d}),\eta^{Q}(x)=\sigma(w^{T}x)\},

for any
\beta,w\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
.
The family of distribution pairs then becomes

\Pi^{LR}=\Pi^{LR}(s,\Pi,M)=\{(Q_{\beta},P_{w}):(\beta,w)\in\Theta(s,\Delta)\},

where
\Theta(s,\Delta)
is defined in (
21
). Moreover, we abbreviate
\mathbb{E}_{\beta}
as
\mathbb{E}_{(X,Y)\sim Q_{\beta}}
, the expectation with respect to a new observation drawn from the distribution
Q_{\beta}
. Similarly, define
\mathcal{E}_{Q_{\beta}}(f)
as the excess risk with respect to the target distribution
Q_{\beta}
.

Before entering into the proof of the main theorems, we first show the following lemma that bounds the ambiguity level for any
(Q,P)\in\Pi^{LR}
:

Lemma 4
.

Assume that
L\leq m\|\beta_{Q}\|_{2}\leq\|\beta_{P}\|_{2}\leq U
for some constant
L,U>0
and
0<m\leq 1
. We have that
\Pi^{LR}(s,\Delta)\subset\Pi(\alpha,C_{\alpha},\gamma,C_{\gamma},\varepsilon)
where

\alpha=1,\ C_{\alpha}=\frac{16m}{\sqrt{2\pi}L}\lor 4,\ \gamma=1,\ C_{\gamma}=\frac{m}{\pi},

\varepsilon(z;1,\frac{m}{\pi})=\left((\frac{16m}{\sqrt{2\pi}L}\lor 4)z^{2}\right)\land\frac{\sqrt{2}U}{m}\Delta^{2}.

D.1
Proof of Lemma
4

For any
x\in B(0,1)
, we have

\sigma^{\prime}(\beta_{Q}^{T}x)=\sigma(\beta_{Q}^{T}x)(1-\sigma(\beta_{Q}^{T}x))\beta_{Q},

\sigma^{\prime}(\beta_{P}^{T}x)=\sigma(\beta_{Q}^{T}x)(1-\sigma(\beta_{Q}^{T}x))\beta_{P}.

Define
e_{Q}
and
e_{P}
as the unit vectors that have the same directions with
\beta_{Q}
and
\beta_{P}
, respectively.

Verify Margin Assumption:
From the simple equality that
\log(1+\frac{4t}{1-2t})\leq 8t
for any
t\in[0,\frac{1}{4}]
and
\|\beta_{Q}\|\geq\frac{L}{m}
we have

\displaystyle Q_{X}(0<|\sigma(\beta_{Q}^{T}X)-\frac{1}{2}|<t)

\displaystyle=Q_{X}(0<|\beta_{Q}^{T}X|\leq\log(1+\frac{4t}{1-2t}))

\displaystyle\leq Q_{X}(0<|\beta_{Q}^{T}X|\leq 8t)

\displaystyle\leq Q_{X}(0<|e_{Q}^{T}X|\leq\frac{8m}{L}t)

\displaystyle=Q_{X}(|N(0,1)|\leq\frac{8m}{L}t)

\displaystyle\leq 16\frac{m}{L}t\phi(0)=\frac{16m}{\sqrt{2\pi}L}t,

where
\phi(\cdot)
is the density function of the univariate standard normal distribution
N(0,1)
.
For
t\in(\frac{1}{4},1]
, we trivially have
Q_{X}(0<|\sigma(\beta_{Q}^{T}X)-\frac{1}{2}|<t)\leq 4t
since
Q_{X}
is a probability measure. Therefore, Assumption holds with
\alpha=1,C_{\alpha}=\frac{16m}{\sqrt{2\pi}L}\lor 4
.

Verify Ambiguity Level:

A trivial well-defined ambiguity level by the margin assumption is

\varepsilon(z;\gamma,\frac{m}{\pi})=C_{\alpha}z^{1+\alpha}=(\frac{16m}{\sqrt{2\pi}L}\lor 4)z^{2}.

Hence, it suffices to show that a well-defined ambiguity level is

\varepsilon(z;1,\frac{m}{\pi})=\frac{\sqrt{2}U}{m}\Delta^{2}.

Without loss of generality and due to the symmetry property of of
N(0,I_{d})
, we could rotate
\beta_{Q}
and
\beta_{P}
at the same time so that

\beta_{Q}=(\|\beta_{Q}\|,0,0,\cdots,0),\quad\beta_{P}=(\|\beta_{P}\|\cos\angle(\beta_{Q},\beta_{P}),\|\beta_{P}\|\sin\angle(\beta_{Q},\beta_{P}),0,0,\cdots,0).

Therefore, we assume that only the first coordinate of
\beta_{Q}
and the first and second coordinates of
\beta_{P}
can be non-zero.

For any
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, we define
d_{Q}(x)
and
d_{P}(x)
as the angle between
(x_{1},x_{2},0,\cdots,0)
and the normal planes of
\beta_{Q}
and
\beta_{P}
, respectively. Note that
d_{Q}(x),d_{P}(x)\in[0,\pi/2]
. Define the area

D:=\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:(\beta_{Q}^{T}x)(\beta_{P}^{T}x)<0\}\cup\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:(\beta_{Q}^{T}x)(\beta_{P}^{T}x)\geq 0,d_{P}(x)<\frac{1}{2}d_{Q}(x)\}

as the region where
\eta^{P}
does not give strong signal relative to
\eta^{Q}
. We see that
D
is a cone centered at origin with angle between the two normal planes equal to
2\angle(\beta_{Q},\beta_{P})\leq 2\Delta
.
In addition, for any
x\in D
, the norm of projection of
x
onto the normal plane of
\beta_{Q}
is less than

(x_{1}^{2}+x_{2}^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}\sin 2\angle(\beta_{Q},\beta_{P})\leq 2(x_{1}^{2}+x_{2}^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}\Delta,

where the simple inequality
\sin 2\angle(\beta_{Q},\beta_{P})\leq 2\angle(\beta_{Q},\beta_{P})
is used.
Therefore,

|\eta^{Q}(x)-\frac{1}{2}|\leq 2\max\{\sigma^{\prime}(z)\}_{z\in\mathbb{R}}\|\beta_{Q}\|(x_{1}^{2}+x_{2}^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}\Delta\leq\frac{U}{2m}(x_{1}^{2}+x_{2}^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}\Delta\quad\forall x\in D,

and

\displaystyle\int_{D}|\eta^{Q}(X)-\frac{1}{2}|dQ_{X}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{U}{2m}\Delta\int_{D}(X_{1}^{2}+X_{2}^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}dQ_{X}

\displaystyle=\frac{U}{2m}\Delta(2\Delta)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}(X_{1}^{2}+X_{2}^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}dQ_{X}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{U}{m}\Delta^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}(X_{1}^{2}+X_{2}^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}dQ_{X}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\sqrt{2}U}{m}\Delta^{2},

since
\Delta\in[0,\frac{\pi}{2}]
and
\mathbb{E}_{Q_{X}}[(X_{1}^{2}+X_{2}^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}]\leq\mathbb{E}_{Q_{X}}[X_{1}^{2}+X_{2}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}=\sqrt{2}
.

On the other hand, if
x\notin D
, we have

\displaystyle|\eta^{P}(x)-\frac{1}{2}|

\displaystyle=|\sigma(\beta_{P}^{T}x)-\frac{1}{2}|

\displaystyle=|\sigma(\|\beta_{P}\|(x_{1}^{2}+x_{2}^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}\sin(d_{P}(x))-\frac{1}{2}|

\displaystyle\geq|\sigma(m\|\beta_{Q}\|(x_{1}^{2}+x_{2}^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}\sin(\frac{1}{2}d_{Q}(x))-\frac{1}{2}|

\displaystyle\geq|\sigma(\frac{m}{\pi}\|\beta_{Q}\|(x_{1}^{2}+x_{2}^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}\sin(d_{Q}(x))-\frac{1}{2}|

\displaystyle\geq|\sigma(\frac{m}{\pi}\beta_{Q}^{T}x)-\frac{1}{2}|

\displaystyle\geq\frac{m}{\pi}|\sigma(\beta_{Q}^{T}x)-\frac{1}{2}|,

given the fact that
\sin(\frac{1}{2}t)\geq\frac{\sin t}{\pi}
on
t\in[0,\frac{\pi}{2}]
and that
|\sigma(t)-\frac{1}{2}|/x
in monotone decreasing on
t\geq 0
.
Therefore, a well-defined ambiguity level is
\varepsilon(z;1,\frac{m}{\pi})=\frac{\sqrt{2}U}{m}\Delta^{2}
.
∎

D.2
Proof of Theorem
24

Define the logistic link function
\psi(x):=\log(1+e^{x})
. It is obvious from straightforward calculation that
\psi^{\prime}(x)=\frac{e^{x}}{e^{x}+1},\psi^{\prime\prime}(x)=\frac{e^{x}}{(e^{x}+1)^{2}}\leq\frac{1}{4}
. Prior to the proof, we present two lemmas that are helpful for deriving the key conditions in Theorem
13
. They serve as the parametric bound of the estimator obtained by the loss function optimization in (
20
). See Section
E.4
for the proof of the two lemmas.

Lemma 5
(Parameter Bound for
\hat{\beta}_{Q}
).

Under the notations and conditions of Theorem
24
, there exists some constant
C_{Q},\kappa_{Q}>0
such that

\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{D}}(\|\hat{\beta}_{Q}-\beta_{Q}\|\geq\kappa_{Q}\sqrt{\frac{s\log d}{n_{Q}}})\leq C_{Q}\left(\frac{s\log d}{n_{Q}}\land\frac{s\log d}{n_{P}}\right).

(68)

Lemma 6
(Parameter Bound for
\hat{\beta}_{P}
).

Under the notations and conditions of Theorem
24
, there exists some constant
C_{P},\kappa_{P}>0
such that

\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{D}}(\|\hat{\beta}_{P}-\beta_{P}\|\geq\kappa_{P}(\sqrt{\frac{s\log d}{n_{P}}}+\Delta))\leq C_{P}\left(\frac{s\log d}{n_{Q}}\land\frac{s\log d}{n_{P}}\right)

(69)

when
\Delta\leq c_{P}
for some constant
c_{P}>0
.

By Lemma
5
, there exist constants
C_{Q},\kappa_{Q}>0
such that

\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{D}_{Q}}(E^{c})\leq C_{Q}(\frac{s\log d}{n_{Q}}\land\frac{s\log d}{n_{P}}),

where we define the event
E:=\{\|\hat{\beta}_{Q}-\beta_{Q}\|\leq\kappa_{Q}\sqrt{\frac{s\log d}{n_{Q}}}\}
.
Define
\hat{e}
as the unit vector with the same direction as
\hat{\beta}_{Q}-\beta_{Q}
. We define

\Omega^{*}:=\{x\in\Omega:|\hat{e}^{T}x|\leq\sqrt{2}\tau/(\frac{\kappa_{Q}}{2\sqrt{2}}\sqrt{\frac{s\log d}{n_{Q}}})\}.

Since
\hat{e}^{T}X\sim N(0,1)
with respect to
Q_{X}
, the tail bound of normal distributions that

Q(\Omega^{*})\geq 1-2\exp(-\left(\tau/(\frac{\kappa_{Q}}{2\sqrt{2}}\sqrt{\frac{s\log d}{n_{Q}}})\right)^{2}).

Since
\sigma(\cdot)
is Lipschitz-continuous with the Lipschitz constant as
\frac{1}{4}
, on the event
E
, we further have

|\sigma(\beta_{Q}^{T}x)-\sigma(\hat{\beta}_{Q}^{T}x)|\leq\frac{1}{4}|(\beta_{Q}^{T}-\hat{\beta}_{Q}^{T})x|\leq\|\hat{\beta}_{Q}-\beta_{Q}\|\cdot|\hat{e}^{T}x|\leq\tau

(70)

for any
x\in\Omega^{*}
.

Now we could directly apply Theorem
13
where the parameters are

\tau=c_{\tau}\sqrt{\frac{s\log d}{n_{Q}}}\log(n_{Q}\lor n_{P}),

and

\delta_{Q}(n_{Q},\tau)=2\exp(-\left(\tau/(\frac{\kappa_{Q}}{2\sqrt{2}}\sqrt{\frac{s\log d}{n_{Q}}})\right)^{2})+C_{Q}(\frac{s\log d}{n_{Q}}\land\frac{s\log d}{n_{P}})

with
c_{\tau}\geq\frac{\kappa_{Q}}{2\sqrt{2}}
. Note that
\delta_{Q}(n_{Q},\tau)\lesssim\frac{s\log d}{n_{Q}}\land\frac{s\log d}{n_{P}}
. By Theorem
13
, the TAB classifier
\hat{f}_{TAB}^{LR}
satisfies

\displaystyle\sup_{(Q,P)\in\Pi^{LR}}\mathbb{E}_{(\mathcal{D}_{Q},\mathcal{D}_{P})}\mathcal{E}_{Q}(\hat{f}^{LR}_{TAB})

\displaystyle\lesssim\sup_{(Q,P)\in\Pi^{LR}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}_{P}}\xi(\mathbf{1}\{\hat{\beta}_{P}^{T}x\geq 0\};1,\frac{m}{\pi})\land\tau^{2}+\varepsilon(2\tau;1,\frac{m}{\pi})+\delta_{Q}(n_{Q},\tau)

\displaystyle\lesssim\sup_{(Q,P)\in\Pi^{LR}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}_{P}}\xi(\mathbf{1}\{\hat{\beta}_{P}^{T}x\geq 0\};1,\frac{m}{\pi})\land(\log^{2}(n_{Q}\lor n_{P})\frac{s\log d}{n_{Q}})

\displaystyle+(\log^{2}(n_{Q}\lor n_{P})\frac{s\log d}{n_{Q}})\land\Delta^{2}

\displaystyle+\frac{s\log d}{n_{Q}}\land\frac{s\log d}{n_{P}}.

Therefore, it suffices to show that

\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}_{P}}\xi(\mathbf{1}\{\hat{\beta}_{P}^{T}x\geq 0\};1,\frac{m}{\pi})\lesssim\frac{s\log d}{n_{P}}+\Delta^{2}

for any
(Q,P)\in\Pi^{LR}
to prove Theorem
24
. If
\Delta>c_{p}
where
c_{p}
is the constant constraint of
\Delta
in Lemma
6
, this bound is trivial as
\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}_{P}}\xi(\mathbf{1}\{\hat{\beta}_{P}^{T}x\geq 0\};1,\frac{m}{\pi})\leq 1
. Hence, we WLOG assume that
\Delta\leq c_{p}
to apply Lemma
6
. In addition, we may as well assume that
c_{P}
is small enough such that
\kappa_{P}c_{P}\leq\frac{L}{4}
.

By Lemma
6
, there exist constants
C_{P},\kappa_{P}>0
such that

\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{D}_{P}}(E_{2}^{c})\leq C_{P}(\frac{s\log d}{n_{Q}}\land\frac{s\log d}{n_{P}}),

where we define the event
E_{2}:=\{\|\hat{\beta}_{P}-\beta_{P}\|\leq\kappa_{P}(\sqrt{\frac{s\log d}{n_{P}}}+\Delta)\}
. Denote
\kappa_{P}(\sqrt{\frac{s\log d}{n_{P}}}+\Delta)
by
\Lambda
by simplicity. By the theorem setting, we could assume that
n_{P}
is large enough such that
\kappa_{P}\sqrt{\frac{s\log d}{n_{P}}}\leq\frac{L}{4}
, so we have
\Lambda\leq\frac{L}{2}\leq\|\beta_{P}\|_{2}
. Therefore, on the event
E_{2}
we have
\angle(\hat{\beta}_{P},\beta_{P})\leq\frac{\pi}{2}
and

\sin\angle(\hat{\beta}_{P},\beta_{P})/\|\hat{\beta}_{P}-\beta_{P}\|\leq 1/\|\beta_{P}\|

by the law of sines. Hence,

\angle(\hat{\beta}_{P},\beta_{P})\leq\frac{\pi}{2}\sin\angle(\hat{\beta}_{P},\beta_{P})\leq\|\hat{\beta}_{P}-\beta_{P}\|/\|\beta_{P}\|\leq\frac{\Lambda}{L}.

Without loss of generality and due to the symmetry property of of
N(0,I_{d})
, we could rotate
\beta_{P}
and
\hat{\beta}_{P}
at the same time so that

\beta_{P}=(\|\beta_{P}\|,0,0,\cdots,0),\quad\hat{\beta}_{P}=(\|\hat{\beta}_{P}\|\cos\angle(\hat{\beta}_{P},\beta_{P}),\|\hat{\beta}_{P}\|\sin\angle(\hat{\beta}_{P},\beta_{P}),0,0,\cdots,0).

Therefore, we assume that only the first coordinate of
\beta_{Q}
and the first and second coordinates of
\hat{\beta}_{P}
can be non-zero.

Define
D:=\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:(\beta_{P}^{T}x)(\hat{\beta}_{P}^{T}x)<0\}
, which is a cone centered at the origin with angle size bounded by
\frac{\Lambda}{L}
. For any
x\in D
, the norm of projection of
x
onto the normal plane of
\beta_{Q}
is less than

(x_{1}^{2}+x_{2}^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}\sin\angle(\hat{\beta}_{P},\beta_{P})\leq(x_{1}^{2}+x_{2}^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}\frac{\Lambda}{L},

where the simple inequality
\sin\angle(\hat{\beta}_{P},\beta_{P})\leq\angle(\hat{\beta}_{P},\beta_{P})
is used.
Therefore,

|\eta^{Q}(x)-\frac{1}{2}|\leq\max\{\sigma^{\prime}(z)\}_{z\in\mathbb{R}}\|\beta_{P}\|(x_{1}^{2}+x_{2}^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}\frac{\Lambda}{L}\leq\frac{U}{4}(x_{1}^{2}+x_{2}^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}\frac{\Lambda}{L}\quad\forall x\in D,

and

\displaystyle\int_{D}|\eta^{Q}(X)-\frac{1}{2}|dQ_{X}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{U}{4}\frac{\Lambda}{L}\int_{D}(X_{1}^{2}+X_{2}^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}dQ_{X}

\displaystyle=\frac{U}{4}(\frac{\Lambda}{L})^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}(X_{1}^{2}+X_{2}^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}dQ_{X}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\sqrt{2}U}{4L^{2}}\Lambda^{2}

since
\frac{\Lambda}{L}\in[0,\frac{\pi}{2}]
and
\mathbb{E}_{Q_{X}}[(X_{1}^{2}+X_{2}^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}]\leq\mathbb{E}_{Q_{X}}[X_{1}^{2}+X_{2}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}=\sqrt{2}
. Hence, we obtain that

\displaystyle\xi(\mathbf{1}\{\hat{\beta}_{P}^{T}x\geq 0\};1,\frac{m}{\pi})

\displaystyle=2\mathbb{E}_{X\sim N(0,I_{d})}[|\eta^{P}-\frac{1}{2}|\mathbf{1}\{X\in D\}]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\sqrt{2}U}{2L^{2}}\Lambda^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{\sqrt{2}U}{2L^{2}}\kappa_{P}^{2}(\sqrt{\frac{s\log d}{n_{P}}}+\Delta)^{2}.

To summarize, we have the bounds
\xi(\mathbf{1}\{\hat{\beta}_{P}^{T}x\geq 0\};1,\frac{m}{\pi})\leq\frac{\Lambda}{L}
on the event
E_{2}
and
\xi(\mathbf{1}\{\hat{\beta}_{P}^{T}x\geq 0\};1,\frac{m}{\pi})\leq 1
on the event
E_{2}^{c}
. As a result,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}_{P}}\xi(\mathbf{1}\{\hat{\beta}_{P}^{T}x\geq 0\};1,\frac{m}{\pi})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}_{P}}[\xi(\mathbf{1}\{\hat{\beta}_{P}^{T}x\geq 0\};1,\frac{m}{\pi})|E_{2}]\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{D}_{P}}(E_{2})+\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}_{P}}[\xi(\mathbf{1}\{\hat{\beta}_{P}^{T}x\geq 0\};1,\frac{m}{\pi})|E_{2}^{c}]\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{D}_{P}}(E_{2}^{c})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\sqrt{2}U}{2L^{2}}\kappa_{P}^{2}(\sqrt{\frac{s\log d}{n_{P}}}+\Delta)^{2}\times 1+1\times C_{P}(\frac{s\log d}{n_{Q}}\land\frac{s\log d}{n_{P}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\sqrt{2}U}{2L^{2}}\kappa_{P}^{2}(\sqrt{\frac{s\log d}{n_{P}}}+\Delta)^{2}+C_{P}(\frac{s\log d}{n_{Q}}\land\frac{s\log d}{n_{P}})

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{s\log d}{n_{P}}+\Delta^{2}.

∎

D.3
Proof of Theorem
7

Given an arbitrary classifier
\hat{f}
based on
\mathcal{D}_{Q}
and
\mathcal{D}_{P}
. Our goal is to show that

\sup_{(Q,P)\in\Pi^{LR}}\mathbb{E}\mathcal{E}_{Q}(\hat{f})\gtrsim\left(\frac{s\log d}{n_{P}}+\Delta^{2}\right)\land\frac{s\log d}{n_{Q}}.

As part of the proof, we would like to first show that

\sup_{(Q,P)\in\Pi^{LR}}\mathbb{E}\mathcal{E}_{Q}(\hat{f})\gtrsim\frac{s\log d}{n_{P}}\land\frac{s\log d}{n_{Q}}.

The idea is to suppose

\beta_{P}=\beta_{Q}=\beta,\quad P=Q=Q_{\beta}

which implies
\angle(\beta_{Q},\beta_{P})=0
, and then reveal the lower bound provided by the combined sample
\mathcal{D}_{Q}\cup\mathcal{D}_{P}
. The proof is based on Fano’s lemma on the vector angle.

We consider a class of
\beta\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
such that

\|\beta\|_{0}\leq s,\ \beta_{1}=1,\ |\beta_{j}|\in\{0,C\sqrt{\log d/(n_{Q}\lor n_{P})},-C\sqrt{\log d/(n_{Q}\lor n_{P})}\}\ (\forall 2\leq j\leq d),

where the constant
C>0
will be specified later. Denote such a class of
\beta
as
\mathcal{H}
. The construction of the class of
\beta
is inspired and similar to
Raskutti et al., 2009
. By Lemma 5 of
Raskutti et al., 2009
, we see that there exists a subset of
\mathcal{H}
, named
\tilde{\mathcal{H}}
, such that

|\tilde{\mathcal{H}}|\geq\exp(\frac{s-1}{2}\log\frac{d-s}{(s-1)/2})

and

\sum_{j=2}^{d}\mathbf{1}\{\beta_{j}\neq\beta_{j}^{\prime}\}\geq\frac{s-1}{2},\quad\forall\beta,\beta^{\prime}\in\tilde{\mathcal{H}}.

We provide the following two observations on the elements of
\tilde{\mathcal{H}}:

•

For any
\beta\in\tilde{\mathcal{H}}
, its norm is bounded by the following inequality:

1\leq\|\beta\|_{2}\leq 1+C\sqrt{s\log d/(n_{Q}\lor n_{P})}\leq M,

(71)

for some
M>0
large enough since we supposed that
s\log d/(n_{Q}\lor n_{P})\lesssim 1
.

•

For any
\beta\neq\beta^{\prime}\in\tilde{\mathcal{H}}
, their angle could be bounded by

\displaystyle\angle(\beta,\beta^{\prime})\geq\sin\angle(\beta,\beta^{\prime})\geq

\displaystyle\frac{(\sum_{j=2}^{d}\mathbf{1}\{\beta_{j}\neq\beta_{j}^{\prime}\})^{\frac{1}{2}}C\sqrt{\log d/(n_{Q}\lor n_{P})}}{\|\beta\|}

(72)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{C\sqrt{s\log d/(n_{Q}\lor n_{P})}}{2M}.

This inequality holds since as long as
\beta_{j}\neq\beta_{j}^{\prime}
for some
2\leq j\leq d
, based on the construction of
\tilde{\mathcal{H}}
we have
|\beta_{j}-\beta_{j}^{\prime}|\geq C\sqrt{\log d/(n_{Q}\lor n_{P})}
.

Next, we define the random variable
Z
as the
\beta\in\tilde{\mathcal{H}}
that attains the minimum of
\mathcal{E}_{Q_{\beta}}(\hat{f})
, i.e.

Z:=\argmin_{\beta\in\tilde{\mathcal{H}}}\mathcal{E}_{Q_{\beta}}(\hat{f}).

For any
\beta^{\prime}\neq Z,\beta^{\prime}\in\tilde{\mathcal{H}}
, by Lemma
10
, we have that

\mathcal{E}_{Q_{Z}}(\hat{f})+\mathcal{E}_{Q_{\beta^{\prime}}}(\hat{f})\geq\frac{\sigma(M)(1-\sigma(M))}{20\pi}\angle(Z,\beta^{\prime})^{2}.

by (
71
). The choice of
Z
further indicates that

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{Q_{\beta^{\prime}}}(\hat{f})\geq

\displaystyle\frac{\sigma(M)(1-\sigma(M))}{40\pi}\angle(Z,\beta^{\prime})^{2}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{C\sigma(M)(1-\sigma(M))}{80\pi M}\sqrt{s\log d/(n_{Q}\lor n_{P})}.

We denote the constant term
\frac{C\sigma(M)(1-\sigma(M))}{80\pi M}
by
C_{M}
for simplicity. Hence,

\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}_{\beta^{\prime}}}\mathcal{E}_{Q_{\beta^{\prime}}}(\hat{f})\geq C_{M}^{2}s\log d/(n_{Q}\lor n_{P})\times\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{D}_{\beta^{\prime}}}(Z\neq\beta^{\prime}),

and so

\displaystyle\sup_{(Q,P)\in\Pi}\mathbb{E}\mathcal{E}_{Q}(\hat{f})\geq

\displaystyle\max_{\beta_{Q}=\beta_{P}=\beta^{\prime}\in\tilde{\mathcal{H}}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}_{\beta^{\prime}}}\mathcal{E}_{Q_{\beta^{\prime}}}(\hat{f})

(73)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle C_{M}^{2}s\log d/(n_{Q}\lor n_{P})\max_{\beta^{\prime}\in\tilde{\mathcal{H}}}\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{D}_{\beta^{\prime}}}(Z\neq\beta^{\prime})

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle C_{M}^{2}s\log d/(n_{Q}\lor n_{P})\frac{1}{|\tilde{\mathcal{H}}|}\sum_{\beta^{\prime}\in\tilde{\mathcal{H}}}\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{D}_{\beta^{\prime}}}(Z\neq\beta^{\prime}).

The remaining work is to lower bound
\frac{1}{|\tilde{\mathcal{H}}|}\sum_{\beta^{\prime}\in\tilde{\mathcal{H}}}\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{D}_{\beta^{\prime}}}(Z\neq\beta^{\prime})
by some positive constant. By Fano’s lemma where the total sample size is
n_{Q}+n_{P}
(Note that we suppose
P=Q
), we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|\tilde{\mathcal{H}}|}\sum_{\beta^{\prime}\in\tilde{\mathcal{H}}}\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{D}_{\beta^{\prime}}}(Z\neq\beta^{\prime})\geq

\displaystyle 1-\frac{\log 2+(n_{Q}+n_{P})\max_{\beta\neq\beta^{\prime}\in\tilde{\mathcal{H}}}KL(Q_{\beta},Q_{\beta^{\prime}})}{\log|\tilde{\mathcal{H}}|}

(74)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle 1-\frac{\log 2+(n_{Q}+n_{P})\max_{\beta\neq\beta^{\prime}\in\tilde{\mathcal{H}}}KL(Q_{\beta},Q_{\beta^{\prime}})}{c\cdot s\log d}

since

\log|\tilde{\mathcal{H}}|\geq\frac{s-1}{2}\log\frac{d-s}{(s-1)/2}\geq c\cdot s\log d
for some
c>0
small enough. For any
\beta\neq\beta^{\prime}\in\tilde{\mathcal{H}}
, their Kullback-Leibler divergence satisfies

\displaystyle KL(Q_{\beta},Q_{\beta^{\prime}})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{Q_{\beta}}[\psi^{\prime\prime}(X^{T}(t\beta+(1-t)\beta^{\prime}))(X^{T}(\beta-\beta^{\prime}))^{2}]

\displaystyle\leq C_{\psi}\mathbb{E}_{Q_{\beta}}[(X^{T}(\beta-\beta^{\prime}))^{2}]

\displaystyle\leq C_{\psi}\|\beta-\beta^{\prime}\|^{2}\leq C_{\psi}C^{2}(s\log d)/(n_{Q}\lor n_{P})

for the logistic regression link function
\psi(u)=\log(1+e^{u})
, some constant
t\in[0,1]
and

C_{\psi}:=\|\psi^{\prime\prime}\|_{\infty}.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\frac{\log 2+(n_{Q}+n_{P})\max_{\beta\neq\beta^{\prime}\in\tilde{\mathcal{H}}}KL(Q_{\beta},Q_{\beta^{\prime}})}{c\cdot s\log d}\leq\frac{\log 2+C_{\psi}C^{2}(s\log d)\frac{(n_{Q}+n_{P})}{n_{Q}\lor n_{P}}}{c\cdot s\log d}\leq\frac{1}{2}

by choosing
C
to be small enough. With such a choice of
C
, we see that (
73
) further reduces to

\displaystyle\sup_{(Q,P)\in\Pi}\mathbb{E}\mathcal{E}_{Q}(\hat{f})\geq

\displaystyle C_{M}^{2}s\log d/(n_{Q}\lor n_{P})\frac{1}{|\tilde{\mathcal{H}}|}\sum_{\beta^{\prime}\in\tilde{\mathcal{H}}}\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{D}_{\beta^{\prime}}}(Z\neq\beta^{\prime})

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}C_{M}^{2}s\log d/(n_{Q}\lor n_{P})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}C_{M}^{2}\frac{s\log d}{n_{Q}}\land\frac{s\log d}{n_{P}},

which is just our desired result.
∎

Next, we would like to show that

\sup_{(Q,P)\in\Pi^{LR}}\mathbb{E}\mathcal{E}_{Q}(\hat{f})\gtrsim\Delta^{2}\land\frac{s\log d}{n_{Q}}.

First, we fix
\beta_{P}=h
in the sense that

h_{1}=1,\quad h_{j}=0\quad(\forall 2\leq j\leq d).

Next, we consider a class of
\beta
such that

\|\beta\|_{0}\leq s,\ \beta_{1}=1,\ |\beta_{j}|\in\{0,C_{\Delta}\sqrt{\log d/n_{Q}}\land\frac{\sin\Delta}{\sqrt{s}},-C_{\Delta}\sqrt{\log d/n_{Q}}\land\frac{\sin\Delta}{\sqrt{s}}\}\ (\forall 2\leq j\leq d),

where the constant
C_{\Delta}\geq 0
will be specified later. Denote such a class of
\beta
as
\mathcal{H}_{\Delta}
.

We want to verify that for any
\beta\in\mathcal{H}_{\Delta},(Q_{\beta},P_{h})\in\Pi^{LR}
. Since it is obvious that
\|\beta\|_{0}\leq s
by definition, it suffices to show that
\angle(\beta,h)\leq\Delta
from the calculation of the angle sine. It holds that

\sin\angle(\beta,h)\leq\frac{(\sum_{j=2}^{d}\mathbf{1}\{\beta\neq h\})^{\frac{1}{2}}(\frac{\sin\Delta}{\sqrt{s}})}{\|h\|}\leq\sin\Delta,

(75)

since
\sum_{j=2}^{d}\mathbf{1}\{\beta\neq h\}\leq s,\|h\|=1
, and
|\beta-h|\leq\frac{\sin\Delta}{\sqrt{s}}
if
\beta_{j}\neq h_{j}
. Also, the dot product satisfies

\beta^{T}h=1>0,

(76)

which indicates that
\angle(\beta,h)<\frac{\pi}{2}
. Based on (
75
) and (
76
), we conclude that

\angle(\beta,h)\leq\Delta,\quad\forall\beta\in\mathcal{H}_{\Delta}.

By following the same idea in the first part of the proof, Lemma 5 of
Raskutti et al., 2009
shows that there exists a subset of
\mathcal{H}_{\Delta}
, named
\tilde{\mathcal{H}}_{\Delta}
, such that

|\tilde{\mathcal{H}}_{\Delta}|\geq\exp(\frac{s-1}{2}\log\frac{d-s}{(s-1)/2})

and

\sum_{j=2}^{d}\mathbf{1}\{\beta_{j}\neq\beta_{j}^{\prime}\}\geq\frac{s-1}{2},\quad\forall\beta,\beta^{\prime}\in\tilde{\mathcal{H}}_{\Delta}.

Following the same procedure of (
71
) and (
72
), we claim that

•

For any
\beta\in\tilde{\mathcal{H}}_{\Delta}
, its norm is bounded by the following inequality:

1\leq\|\beta\|_{2}\leq 1+C_{\Delta}\sin\Delta\leq 1+C_{\Delta}.

(77)

•

For any
\beta\neq\beta^{\prime}\in\tilde{\mathcal{H}}_{\Delta}
, their angle sine could be bounded by

\displaystyle\angle(\beta,\beta^{\prime})\geq\sin\angle(\beta,\beta^{\prime})\geq

\displaystyle\frac{(\sum_{j=2}^{d}\mathbf{1}\{\beta_{j}\neq\beta_{j}^{\prime}\})^{\frac{1}{2}}(C_{\Delta}\sqrt{\log d/n_{Q}}\land\frac{\sin\Delta}{\sqrt{s}})}{\|\beta\|}

(78)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{C_{\Delta}\sqrt{s\log d/n_{Q}}\land\sin\Delta}{2(1+C_{\Delta})}.

This inequality holds since as long as
\beta_{j}\neq\beta_{j}^{\prime}
for some
2\leq j\leq d
, based on the construction of
\tilde{\mathcal{H}}_{\Delta}
we have
|\beta_{j}-\beta_{j}^{\prime}|\geq C_{\Delta}\sqrt{\log d/n_{Q}}\land\frac{\sin\Delta}{\sqrt{s}}
.

Define the random variable
Z
as the
\beta\in\tilde{\mathcal{H}}_{\Delta}
that attains the minimum of
\mathcal{E}_{Q_{\beta}}(\hat{f})
, i.e.

Z:=\argmin_{\beta\in\tilde{\mathcal{H}}_{\Delta}}\mathcal{E}_{Q_{\beta}}(\hat{f}).

For any
\beta^{\prime}\neq Z,\beta^{\prime}\in\tilde{\mathcal{H}}_{\Delta}
, by Lemma
10
, we have that

\mathcal{E}_{Q_{Z}}(\hat{f})+\mathcal{E}_{Q_{\beta^{\prime}}}(\hat{f})\geq\frac{\sigma(1+C_{\Delta})(1-\sigma(1+C_{\Delta}))}{20\pi}\angle(Z,\beta^{\prime})^{2}.

by (
77
). The choice of
Z
further indicates that

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{Q_{\beta^{\prime}}}(\hat{f})\geq

\displaystyle\frac{\sigma(1+C_{\Delta})(1-\sigma(1+C_{\Delta}))}{40\pi}\angle(Z,\beta^{\prime})^{2}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{\sigma(1+C_{\Delta})(1-\sigma(1+C_{\Delta}))}{80\pi(1+C_{\Delta})}(C_{\Delta}^{2}s\log d/n_{Q})\land\sin^{2}\Delta.

We denote the constant term
\frac{\sigma(1+C_{\Delta})(1-\sigma(1+C_{\Delta}))}{80\pi}
by
C^{\prime}_{\Delta}
for simplicity. Hence,

\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}_{\beta^{\prime}}}\mathcal{E}_{Q_{\beta^{\prime}}}(\hat{f})\geq C^{\prime}_{\Delta}((C_{\Delta}^{2}s\log d/n_{Q})\land\sin^{2}\Delta)\times\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{D}_{\beta^{\prime}}}(Z\neq\beta^{\prime}),

and so

\displaystyle\sup_{(Q,P)\in\Pi}\mathbb{E}\mathcal{E}_{Q}(\hat{f})\geq

\displaystyle\max_{\beta^{\prime}\in\tilde{\mathcal{H}}_{\Delta},\beta_{P}=h}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}_{\beta^{\prime}}}\mathcal{E}_{Q_{\beta^{\prime}}}(\hat{f})

(79)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle C^{\prime}_{\Delta}((C_{\Delta}^{2}s\log d/n_{Q})\land\sin^{2}\Delta)\max_{\beta^{\prime}\in\tilde{\mathcal{H}}_{\Delta}}\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{D}_{\beta^{\prime}}}(Z\neq\beta^{\prime})

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle C^{\prime}_{\Delta}((C_{\Delta}^{2}s\log d/n_{Q})\land\sin^{2}\Delta)\frac{1}{|\tilde{\mathcal{H}}_{\Delta}|}\sum_{\beta^{\prime}\in\tilde{\mathcal{H}}_{\Delta}}\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{D}_{\beta^{\prime}}}(Z\neq\beta^{\prime}).

By Fano’s lemma where the total sample size is
n_{Q}
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|\tilde{\mathcal{H}}_{\Delta}|}\sum_{\beta^{\prime}\in\tilde{\mathcal{H}}_{\Delta}}\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{D}_{\beta^{\prime}}}(Z\neq\beta^{\prime})\geq

\displaystyle 1-\frac{\log 2+n_{Q}\max_{\beta\neq\beta^{\prime}\in\tilde{\mathcal{H}}_{\Delta}}KL(Q_{\beta},Q_{\beta^{\prime}})}{\log|\tilde{\mathcal{H}}|}

(80)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle 1-\frac{\log 2+n_{Q}\max_{\beta\neq\beta^{\prime}}KL(Q_{\beta},Q_{\beta^{\prime}})}{c\cdot s\log d}

since

\log|\tilde{\mathcal{H}}_{\Delta}|\geq\frac{s-1}{2}\log\frac{d-s}{(s-1)/2}\geq c\cdot s\log d
for some
c>0
small enough. For any
\beta\neq\beta^{\prime}\in\tilde{\mathcal{H}}_{\Delta}
, their Kullback-Leibler divergence satisfies

\displaystyle KL(Q_{\beta},Q_{\beta^{\prime}})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{Q_{\beta}}[\psi^{\prime\prime}(X^{T}(t\beta+(1-t)\beta^{\prime}))(X^{T}(\beta-\beta^{\prime}))^{2}]

\displaystyle\leq C_{\psi}\mathbb{E}_{Q_{\beta}}[(X^{T}(\beta-\beta^{\prime}))^{2}]

\displaystyle\leq C_{\psi}\|\beta-\beta^{\prime}\|^{2}\leq C_{\psi}C_{\Delta}^{2}\frac{s\log d}{n_{Q}}

for the logistic regression link function
\psi(u)=\log(1+e^{u})
and some constants
t\in[0,1]
. Therefore,

\displaystyle\frac{\log 2+(n_{Q}+n_{P})\max_{\beta\neq\beta^{\prime}}KL(Q_{\beta},Q_{\beta^{\prime}})}{c\cdot s\log d}\leq\frac{\log 2+C_{\psi}C_{\Delta}^{2}s\log d}{c\cdot s\log d}\leq\frac{1}{2}

by choosing
C_{\Delta}
to be small enough. With such a choice of
C_{\Delta}
, we see that (
73
) further reduces to

\displaystyle\sup_{(Q,P)\in\Pi}\mathbb{E}\mathcal{E}_{Q}(\hat{f})\geq

\displaystyle C^{\prime}_{\Delta}((C_{\Delta}^{2}s\log d/n_{Q})\land\sin^{2}\Delta)\max_{\beta^{\prime}\in\tilde{\mathcal{H}}_{\Delta}}\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{D}_{\beta^{\prime}}}(Z\neq\beta^{\prime})

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}C^{\prime}_{\Delta}((C_{\Delta}^{2}s\log d/n_{Q})\land\sin^{2}\Delta)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}C^{\prime}_{\Delta}C_{\Delta}^{2}\frac{s\log d}{n_{Q}}\land C^{\prime}_{\Delta}\sin^{2}\Delta

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1}{2}C^{\prime}_{\Delta}C_{\Delta}^{2}\frac{s\log d}{n_{Q}}\right)\land\left(\frac{4C^{\prime}_{\Delta}}{\pi^{2}}\Delta^{2}\right),

which is just our desired result.

∎Theorem
7
is achieved simply by combining the first and second parts of the proof above.

E
Auxiliary Results

E.1
Proof of Proposition
2

In this subsection,
\varepsilon(z;\gamma,C_{\gamma})\equiv 0
in the parameter setting of
\Pi^{NP}
.

The direction of
\{Q:(Q,P)\in\Pi^{NP}\}\subset\Pi_{Q}^{NP}
is obvious from the definitions.
Suppose
Q\in\Pi^{NP}_{Q}
. We always assume that
P_{X}=Q_{X}
in this part. It is then obvious that
P_{X}=Q_{X}\in\mathcal{S}(\mu^{+},\mu^{-},c_{\mu},r_{\mu})
.

If
\beta=0
, then
\beta_{P}=0
, and
\eta^{Q},\eta^{P}
can be noncontinuous. Simply setting

\eta^{P}=\frac{1}{2}+\frac{C_{\gamma}}{2^{\gamma}}\mathrm{sgn}\left(\eta^{Q}-\frac{1}{2}\right)

satisfies
(Q,P)\in\Pi^{NP}
provided that
C_{\beta_{P}}\geq\frac{C_{\gamma}}{2^{\gamma}}
.

If
\beta>0
, and
\eta^{Q}
does not hit
\frac{1}{2}
on
\Omega
, then the continuity of
\eta^{Q}
indicates that
\mathrm{sgn}(\eta^{Q}-\frac{1}{2})
is a fixed constant. In this case, either setting
\eta^{P}\equiv 1
or
\eta^{P}\equiv 0
satisfies
(Q,P)\in\Pi^{NP}
.

At last, we prove the first statement under the conditions that
\beta>0
and
\eta^{Q}
hits
\frac{1}{2}
on
\Omega
. If
\beta_{P}=0
, then we can again set
\eta^{P}=\frac{1}{2}+\frac{C_{\gamma}}{2^{\gamma}}\mathrm{sgn}\left(\eta^{Q}-\frac{1}{2}\right)
to satisfy that
(Q,P)\in\Pi^{NP}
, so we consider
\beta_{P}>0
below.

For any
x\in\Omega
, define
d(x)
as the point
x^{\prime}\in\Omega,\eta^{Q}(x^{\prime})=\frac{1}{2}
that is closest to
x
, i.e.

d(x):=\argmin_{x^{\prime}\in\Omega,\eta^{Q}(x^{\prime})=\frac{1}{2}}\|x-x^{\prime}\|.

This function is well-defined since
\eta^{Q}
hits
\frac{1}{2}
on
\Omega
. Define
\eta^{P}
as

\eta^{P}=\frac{1}{2}+C\mathrm{sgn}\left(\eta^{Q}-\frac{1}{2}\right)\|x-d(x)\|^{\beta_{P}},

where the constant
C>0
will be specified later.

By the definition of
P
, we have
(\eta^{P}-\frac{1}{2})(\eta^{Q}-\frac{1}{2})\geq 0
. Moreover, we have

\displaystyle|\eta^{P}(x)-\frac{1}{2}|

\displaystyle\geq C\|x-d(x)\|^{\beta_{P}}

\displaystyle\geq CC_{\beta}^{-\frac{\beta_{P}}{\beta}}\left(C_{\beta}\|x-d(x)\|^{\beta}\right)^{\frac{\beta_{P}}{\beta}}

\displaystyle\geq CC_{\beta}^{-\frac{\beta_{P}}{\beta}}|\eta^{Q}(x)-\frac{1}{2}|^{\frac{\beta_{P}}{\beta}}

\displaystyle\geq CC_{\beta}^{-\frac{\beta_{P}}{\beta}}2^{\beta_{P}/\beta-\gamma}|\eta^{Q}(x)-\frac{1}{2}|^{\gamma}

\displaystyle\geq C_{\gamma}|\eta^{Q}(x)-\frac{1}{2}|^{\gamma}.

We conclude that
\Omega^{+}(\gamma,C_{\gamma})=\Omega
provided that
C\geq C_{\gamma}C_{\beta}^{\frac{\beta_{P}}{\beta}}2^{\gamma-\beta_{P}/\beta}
. Hence, it suffices to obtain that

\eta^{P}\in\mathcal{H}(\beta_{P},C_{\beta_{P}})

to show
(Q,P)\in\Pi^{NP}(\alpha,C_{\alpha},\gamma,C_{\gamma},0,\beta,\beta_{P},C_{\beta},C_{\beta_{P}},\mu^{+},\mu^{-},c_{\mu},r_{\mu})
. For any
x,x^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, we assume that
\|x-d(x)\|\geq\|x^{\prime}-d(x^{\prime})\|
without loss of generality. If
(\eta^{Q}(x)-\frac{1}{2})(\eta^{Q}(x^{\prime})-\frac{1}{2})\geq 0
, we have

\displaystyle|\eta^{P}(x)-\eta^{P}(x^{\prime})|

\displaystyle\leq C\||x-d(x)\|^{\beta_{P}}-\|x^{\prime}-d(x^{\prime})\|^{\beta_{P}}|

\displaystyle\leq C|\|x-d(x)||-||x^{\prime}-d(x^{\prime})\||^{\beta_{P}}

\displaystyle\leq C\|x-x^{\prime}\|^{\beta_{P}},

so
\eta^{P}\in\mathcal{H}(\beta_{P},C_{\beta_{P}})
if
C_{\beta_{P}}\geq C
.
If
(\eta^{Q}(x)-\frac{1}{2})(\eta^{Q}(x^{\prime})-\frac{1}{2})<0
, since
\eta^{P}
is continuous, there exists
\lambda\in(0,1)
such that

\eta^{Q}(\lambda x+(1-\lambda)x^{\prime})=\frac{1}{2}.

We have

\displaystyle|\eta^{P}(x)-\eta^{P}(x^{\prime})|

\displaystyle\leq 2C\|x-d(x)\|^{\beta_{P}}

\displaystyle\leq 2C\|x-(\lambda x+(1-\lambda)x^{\prime})\|^{\beta_{P}}

\displaystyle\leq 2C\|x-x^{\prime}\|^{\beta_{P}},

so
\eta^{P}\in\mathcal{H}(\beta_{P},C_{\beta_{P}})
if
C_{\beta_{P}}\geq 2C
. Combining all cases above, we conclude that for any
Q\in\Pi^{NP}_{Q}
, there exists some
P
such that
(Q,P)\in\Pi^{NP}
provided that

C_{\beta_{P}}\geq\max\{C_{\gamma}2^{-\gamma},2C_{\gamma}C_{\beta}^{\frac{\beta_{P}}{\beta}}2^{\gamma-\beta_{P}/\beta}\}.

∎

Let
\Omega=[0,1]^{d}
. Since
\beta_{P}>\gamma\beta\geq 0
,
\eta^{P}
is continuous.
Define

\eta^{Q}=\frac{1}{2}+C_{\beta}(x_{1}-\frac{1}{2})^{\beta}.

When
x_{1}=\frac{1}{2}
for any
x\in\Omega
, it holds that
\eta^{Q}(x)=\frac{1}{2}
. Define
\Omega_{1}=\{x\in\Omega:x_{1}=\frac{1}{2}\}
. We claim
\eta^{P}=\frac{1}{2}
on
x\in\Omega_{1}
. Otherwise, it is trivial to see that there will be a small ball in
\Omega
on which
(\eta^{P}-\frac{1}{2})(\eta^{Q}-\frac{1}{2})<0
, which contradicts the fact that
\varepsilon(z;\gamma,C_{\gamma})\equiv 0
.

Define the unit vector on the first coordinate as
e_{1}:=(1,0,0,\cdots,0)
. We see that for any
t\in[0,1]
,

\eta^{Q}(e_{1})=\frac{1}{2},\quad\eta^{Q}(te_{1})=\frac{1}{2}+C_{\beta}(t-\frac{1}{2})^{\beta},

\eta^{P}(e_{1})=\frac{1}{2},\quad|\eta^{P}(te_{1})-\frac{1}{2}|\geq C_{\gamma}|\eta^{Q}(te_{1})-\frac{1}{2}|^{\gamma}\geq C_{\gamma}C^{\gamma}_{\beta}|t-\frac{1}{2}|^{\gamma\beta}.

(81)

However, since
\eta^{P}\in\mathcal{H}(\beta_{P},C_{\beta_{P}})
, we have

|\eta^{P}(te_{1})-\frac{1}{2}|=|\eta^{P}(te_{1})-\eta^{P}(e_{1})|\leq C_{\beta_{P}}t^{\beta_{P}},

which contradicts with (
81
) by choosing
t>0
to be small enough.
∎

E.2
Proof of Proposition
3

The proof is similar to the proof of Theorem 3.2 in
Cai and Wei, 2021
, which shows that

\inf_{\hat{f}}\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}(Q,P)\in\Pi^{NP}_{0}\\
\Omega=\Omega_{P},\beta_{P}=0,C_{\beta_{P}}=1\end{subarray}}\mathbb{E}\mathcal{E}_{Q}(\hat{f})\\
\gtrsim n_{P}^{-\frac{\beta(1+\alpha)}{2\gamma\beta+d}}\land n_{Q}^{-\frac{\beta(1+\alpha)}{2\beta+d}}.

(82)

without imposing any smoothness condition on
\eta^{P}
. Our objective is to show that the minimax lower bound (
82
) could apply to a broader class:

\inf_{\hat{f}}\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}(Q,P)\in\Pi^{NP}_{0}\\
\gamma\beta\geq\beta_{P}\end{subarray}}\mathbb{E}\mathcal{E}_{Q}(\hat{f})\\
\gtrsim n_{P}^{-\frac{\beta(1+\alpha)}{2\gamma\beta+d}}\land n_{Q}^{-\frac{\beta(1+\alpha)}{2\beta+d}}

with some modifications on the original proof. The only extra work is to verify that our construction
P_{\sigma},\ \sigma\in\{0,1\}^{m}
also satisfies the smoothness condition for
\eta^{P}
with parameters
(\beta_{P},C_{\beta_{P}})
, i.e.,
(Q,P)\in\mathcal{H}(\beta,\beta_{P},C_{\beta},C_{\beta_{P}})
, given that
\beta_{P}<\gamma\beta
.

Define the quantities

r=c_{r}(n_{P}^{-\frac{1}{2\gamma\beta+d}}\land n_{Q}^{-\frac{1}{2\beta+d}}),\quad w=c_{w}r^{d},\quad m=\lfloor c_{m}r^{\alpha\beta-d}\rfloor,

where the constants
c_{r},c_{w},c_{m}
will be specified later in the proof.

We consider a packing
\{x_{k}\}_{k=1,\cdots,m}
with radius
2r
in
[0,1]^{d}
. For an example of such construction, we divide
[0,1]^{d}
into uniform small cubes with side length as
6r
, which forms a grid with at least
\lfloor(6r)^{-1}\rfloor^{d}
small cubes. Since
m\ll\lfloor(6r)^{-1}\rfloor^{d}
, we suppose that
c_{m}
is small enough such that
m<\lfloor(6r)^{-1}\rfloor^{d}
. Therefore, we could assign exactly one sphere with radius
2r
in one cube without intersection, which forms a packing of
\{x_{k}\}_{k=1,\cdots,m}
with radius
2r
in
[0,1]^{d}
. Also, define
B_{c}
as the compliment of these
m
balls, i.e.

B_{c}:=[0,1]^{d}/\left(\bigcup_{k=1}^{m}B(x_{k},2r)\right)
.

For any
\sigma\in\{1,-1\}^{m}
, we consider the regression functions
\eta^{Q}_{\sigma}(x)
and
\eta^{P}_{\sigma}(x)
defined as follows:

\eta^{Q}_{\sigma}(x)=\begin{cases}\frac{1}{2}+\sigma_{k}C_{\beta}r^{\beta}g^{\beta}(\frac{\|x-x_{k}\|}{r})\quad&\text{if $x\in B(x_{k},2r)$ for some $k=1,\cdots,m$,}\\
\frac{1}{2}\quad&\text{otherwise,}\end{cases}

\eta^{P}_{\sigma}(x)=\begin{cases}\frac{1}{2}+\sigma_{k}(C_{\beta_{P}}\lor C_{\gamma}C_{\beta}^{\gamma})r^{\gamma\beta}g^{\gamma\beta}(\frac{\|x-x_{k}\|}{r})\quad&\text{if $x\in B(x_{k},2r)$ for some $k=1,\cdots,m$,}\\
\frac{1}{2}\quad&\text{otherwise,}\end{cases}

where the function
g(\cdot)
is defined as
g(x)=\min\{1,2-x\}
on
x\in[0,2]
.

The construction of the marginal distributions
Q_{\sigma,X}
and
P_{\sigma,X}
is as follows. Let
Q_{\sigma,X}=P_{\sigma,X}
, both of which have the density function
\mu(\cdot)
defined as

\mu(x)=\begin{cases}\frac{w}{\lambda[B(x_{k},r)]}\quad&\text{if $x\in B(x_{k},r)$ for some $k=1,\cdots,m$,}\\
\frac{1-mw}{1-m\lambda[B(x_{k},2r)]}\quad&\text{if $x\in B_{c}$,}\\
0\quad&\text{otherwise.}\end{cases}

Given the the construction of
(Q_{\sigma},P_{\sigma})
, we next verify that
(Q_{\sigma},P_{\sigma})
belongs to
\Pi^{NP}_{0}
for any
\sigma\in\{1,-1\}^{m}
. The fact that
\Omega^{+}(\gamma,C_{\gamma})=\Omega
is trivial by the construction of the regression functions
\eta^{Q}_{\sigma}(x)
and
\eta^{P}_{\sigma}(x)
, so we omit its verification.

Verify Margin Assumption:
We have that

\displaystyle Q_{\sigma}(0<|\eta_{\sigma}^{Q}-\frac{1}{2}|<t)

\displaystyle=mQ_{\sigma}(0<C_{\beta}r^{\beta}g^{\beta}(\frac{\|X-x_{1}\|}{r})\leq t)

\displaystyle=mQ_{\sigma}(0<g(\frac{\|X-x_{1}\|}{r})\leq(\frac{t}{C_{\beta}r^{\beta}})^{\frac{1}{\beta}})

\displaystyle=mw\mathbf{1}\{t\geq C_{\beta}r^{\beta}\}

\displaystyle\leq c_{m}c_{w}r^{\alpha\beta}\mathbf{1}\{t\geq C_{\beta}r^{\beta}\}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\alpha}t^{\alpha}

given that
c_{m}
is small enough. Therefore,
Q_{\sigma}\in\mathcal{M}(\alpha,C_{\alpha})
.

Verify Smoothness Assumption:

It is easy to see that for any
a,b\in[0,2]
we have

|g^{\beta}(a)-g^{\beta}(b)|\leq|a-b|^{\beta}

and

|g^{\gamma\beta}(a)-g^{\gamma\beta}(b)|\leq\begin{cases}|a-b|^{\gamma\beta}\quad&\gamma\beta\leq 1,\\
1-(1-|a-b|)^{\gamma\beta}\leq\gamma\beta|a-b|\quad&\gamma\beta>1.\end{cases}

Thus, for any
x,x^{\prime}\in B(x_{k},2r)
, we obtain from the triangular inequality
\|x-x_{k}\|-\|x^{\prime}-x_{k}\|\leq\|x-x^{\prime}\|
that

|r^{\beta}g^{\beta}(\|x-x_{k}\|/r))-r^{\beta}g^{\beta}(\|x^{\prime}-x_{k}\|/r))|\leq\|x-x^{\prime}\|^{\beta}

(83)

and

|r^{\gamma\beta}g^{\gamma\beta}(\|x-x_{k}\|/r))-r^{\gamma\beta}g^{\gamma\beta}(\|x^{\prime}-x_{k}\|/r))|\leq\begin{cases}\|x-x^{\prime}\|^{\gamma\beta}\quad&\gamma\beta\leq 1,\\
\gamma\beta\cdot r^{\gamma\beta}\|x-x^{\prime}\|\quad&\gamma\beta>1.\end{cases}

(84)

Suppose that
c_{r}
is small enough such that for any
n_{Q},n_{P}\geq 1
, we have

\max\{\gamma\beta\cdot r^{\gamma\beta}(4r)^{1-\beta_{P}},(4r)^{\gamma\beta-\beta_{P}}\}\leq 1,

then we further deduces that

\displaystyle\|x-x^{\prime}\|^{\gamma\beta}\leq\|x-x^{\prime}\|^{\beta_{P}}(4r)^{\gamma\beta-\beta_{P}}\leq\|x-x^{\prime}\|^{\beta_{P}}

(85)

\displaystyle\gamma\beta\cdot r^{\gamma\beta}\|x-x^{\prime}\|\leq\gamma\beta\cdot r^{\gamma\beta}(4r)^{1-\beta_{P}}\|x-x^{\prime}\|^{\beta_{P}}\leq\|x-x^{\prime}\|^{\beta_{P}}.

On one hand, by the definition of
\eta^{Q}_{\sigma}
and (
83
), we see that

|\eta^{Q}_{\sigma}(x)-\eta^{Q}_{\sigma}(x^{\prime})|\leq C_{\beta}\|x-x^{\prime}\|^{\beta},\quad\forall x,x\in[0,1]^{d}.

One the other hand, by the definition of
\eta^{P}_{\sigma}
, (
84
) and (
85
), we see that

|\eta^{P}_{\sigma}(x)-\eta^{P}_{\sigma}(x^{\prime})|\leq C_{\beta_{P}}\|x-x^{\prime}\|^{\gamma\beta}\leq C_{\beta_{P}}\|x-x^{\prime}\|^{\beta_{P}},\quad\forall x,x\in[0,1]^{d}.

Hence,
(Q,P)\in\mathcal{H}(\beta,\beta_{P},C_{\beta},C_{\beta_{P}})
.

Verify Strong Density Condition:

If
x\in B(x_{k},r)
for some
k=1,\cdots,m
, we have

\mu(x)=\frac{w}{\lambda[B(x_{k},r)]}=c_{w}/\pi_{d}.

If
x\in B_{c}
, we have

\mu(x)=\frac{1-c_{w}\lfloor c_{m}r^{\alpha\beta-d}\rfloor r^{d}}{1-2^{d}\pi_{d}\lfloor c_{m}r^{\alpha\beta-d}\rfloor r^{d}}\overset{n_{Q}\rightarrow\infty}{\longrightarrow}1.

Therefore, we could set
c_{w}\in[\pi_{d}\mu^{-},\pi_{d}\mu^{+}]
to satisfy the condition
(Q_{\sigma},P_{\sigma})\in\mathcal{S}(\mu^{+},\mu^{-},c_{\mu},r_{\mu})
.

Putting all verification steps above together, we conclude that
(Q_{\sigma},P_{\sigma})\in\Pi^{NP}_{0}
. We finish the proof of this part by applying Assouad’s lemma to
(Q_{\sigma},P_{\sigma}),\ \forall\sigma\in\{1,-1\}^{m}
.

If
\sigma,\sigma^{\prime}\in\{1,-1\}^{m}
differ only at one coordinate, i.e.

\sigma_{k}=-\sigma^{\prime}_{k},\quad\sigma_{l}=\sigma^{\prime}_{l}\ (\forall l\neq k),

we have the Hellinger distance bound as

\displaystyle H^{2}(Q_{\sigma},Q_{\sigma^{\prime}})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\int\left(\sqrt{\eta^{Q}_{\sigma}(X)}-\sqrt{\eta^{Q}_{\sigma^{\prime}}(X)}\right)^{2}+\left(\sqrt{1-\eta^{Q}_{\sigma}(X)}-\sqrt{1-\eta^{Q}_{\sigma^{\prime}}(X)}\right)^{2}dQ_{X}

\displaystyle=\int_{B(x_{k},r)}\frac{w}{\lambda[B(x_{k},r)]}\left(\sqrt{\frac{1}{2}+C_{\beta}r^{\beta}}-\sqrt{\frac{1}{2}-C_{\beta}r^{\beta}}\right)^{2}dx

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}w(1-\sqrt{1-2C^{2}_{\beta}r^{2\beta}})

\displaystyle\leq C^{2}_{\beta}wr^{2\beta},

\displaystyle H^{2}(P_{\sigma},P_{\sigma^{\prime}})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\int\left(\sqrt{\eta^{P}_{\sigma}(X)}-\sqrt{\eta^{P}_{\sigma^{\prime}}(X)}\right)^{2}+\left(\sqrt{1-\eta^{P}_{\sigma}(X)}-\sqrt{1-\eta^{P}_{\sigma^{\prime}}(X)}\right)^{2}dP_{X}

\displaystyle=\int_{B(x_{k},r)}\frac{w}{\lambda[B(x_{k},r)]}\left(\sqrt{\frac{1}{2}+(C_{\beta_{P}}\lor C_{\gamma}C_{\beta}^{\gamma})r^{\gamma\beta}}-\sqrt{\frac{1}{2}-(C_{\beta_{P}}\lor C_{\gamma}C_{\beta}^{\gamma})r^{\gamma\beta}}\right)^{2}dx

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}w(1-\sqrt{1-2(C^{2}_{\beta_{P}}\lor C^{2}_{\gamma}C_{\beta}^{2\gamma})r^{2\gamma\beta}})

\displaystyle\leq(C^{2}_{\beta_{P}}\lor C^{2}_{\gamma}C_{\beta}^{2\gamma})r^{2\gamma\beta}.

Recall that
r=c_{r}(n_{P}^{-\frac{1}{2\gamma\beta+d}}\land n_{Q}^{-\frac{1}{2\beta+d}})
. By the property of Hellinger distance, we have

\displaystyle H^{2}(\mathbb{P}^{\sigma}_{\mathcal{D}_{Q}}\times\mathbb{P}^{\sigma}_{\mathcal{D}_{P}},\mathbb{P}^{\sigma^{\prime}}_{\mathcal{D}_{Q}}\times\mathbb{P}^{\sigma^{\prime}}_{\mathcal{D}_{P}})\leq

\displaystyle n_{Q}H^{2}(Q_{\sigma},Q_{\sigma^{\prime}})+n_{P}H^{2}(P_{\sigma},P_{\sigma^{\prime}})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C^{2}_{\beta}wn_{Q}r^{2\beta}+(C^{2}_{\beta_{P}}\lor C^{2}_{\gamma}C_{\beta}^{2\gamma})n_{P}r^{2\gamma\beta}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C^{2}_{\beta}c_{r}^{2\beta+d}c_{w}+(C^{2}_{\beta_{P}}\lor C^{2}_{\gamma}C_{\beta}^{2\gamma})c_{r}^{2\gamma\beta+d}c_{w}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\sqrt{2}}{4}

provided that
c_{r}
is small enough. This further indicates that

TV(\mathbb{P}^{\sigma}_{\mathcal{D}_{Q}}\times\mathbb{P}^{\sigma}_{\mathcal{D}_{P}},\mathbb{P}^{\sigma^{\prime}}_{\mathcal{D}_{Q}}\times\mathbb{P}^{\sigma^{\prime}}_{\mathcal{D}_{P}})\leq\sqrt{2}H^{2}(\mathbb{P}^{\sigma}_{\mathcal{D}_{Q}}\times\mathbb{P}^{\sigma}_{\mathcal{D}_{P}},\mathbb{P}^{\sigma^{\prime}}_{\mathcal{D}_{Q}}\times\mathbb{P}^{\sigma^{\prime}}_{\mathcal{D}_{P}})\leq\frac{1}{2}.

(86)

For any empirical classifier
\hat{f}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{Q_{\sigma}}(\hat{f})+\mathcal{E}_{Q_{\sigma^{\prime}}}(\hat{f})

\displaystyle=2\mathbb{E}_{Q_{\sigma}}[|\eta^{Q_{\sigma}}(X)-\frac{1}{2}|\mathbf{1}\{\hat{f}(X)\neq f^{*}_{Q_{\sigma}}(X)\}]

\displaystyle+2\mathbb{E}_{Q_{\sigma^{\prime}}}[|\eta^{Q_{\sigma^{\prime}}}(X)-\frac{1}{2}|\mathbf{1}\{\hat{f}(X)\neq f^{*}_{Q_{\sigma^{\prime}}}(X)\}]

\displaystyle\geq 2\int_{B(x_{k},r)}\frac{w}{\lambda[B(x_{k},r)]}C_{\beta}r^{\beta}(\mathbf{1}\{\hat{f}(X)\neq f^{*}_{Q_{\sigma}}(X)\}+\mathbf{1}\{\hat{f}(X)\neq f^{*}_{Q_{\sigma^{\prime}}}(X)\})dx

\displaystyle=2C_{\beta}wr^{\beta}.

Combining this lower bound with (
86
), the Assouad’s lemma shows that

\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}(Q,P)\in\Pi^{NP}_{0}\\
\Omega=\Omega_{P},\gamma\beta\geq\beta_{P}\end{subarray}}\mathbb{E}\mathcal{E}_{Q}(\hat{f})\geq\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}(Q_{\sigma},P_{\sigma})\\
\sigma\in\{1,-1\}^{m}\end{subarray}}\mathbb{E}\mathcal{E}_{Q_{\sigma}}(\hat{f})\geq\frac{1}{2}C_{\beta}mwr^{\beta}\gtrsim r^{\beta(1+\alpha)}\gtrsim n_{P}^{-\frac{\beta(1+\alpha)}{2\gamma\beta+d}}\land n_{Q}^{-\frac{\beta(1+\alpha)}{2\beta+d}}.

∎

E.3
Proof of Proposition
4

Our objective is to show that the minimax lower bound satisfies

\inf_{\hat{f}}\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}(Q,P)\in\Pi^{NP}_{0}\\
\gamma\beta<\beta_{P}\end{subarray}}\mathbb{E}\mathcal{E}_{Q}(\hat{f})\\
\gtrsim n_{P}^{-\frac{\beta_{P}(1+\alpha)/\gamma}{2\beta_{P}+d}}\land n_{Q}^{-\frac{\beta_{P}(1+\alpha)/\gamma}{2\beta_{P}/\gamma+d}}.

Define the quantities

r_{Q}=c_{r}(n_{P}^{-\frac{\beta_{P}/\gamma\beta}{2\beta_{P}+d}}\land n_{Q}^{-\frac{1}{2\beta+d\gamma\beta/\beta_{P}}}),\quad r_{P}=r_{Q}^{\gamma\beta/\beta_{P}}=c_{r}^{\gamma\beta/\beta_{P}}(n_{P}^{-\frac{1}{2\beta_{P}+d}}\land n_{Q}^{-\frac{1}{2\beta_{P}/\gamma+d}}),

w=c_{w}r_{Q}^{d},\quad m_{0}=\lfloor c_{m_{0}}\frac{r_{P}^{d}}{r_{Q}^{d}}\rfloor,\quad m=\lfloor c_{m}r_{P}^{\alpha\beta_{P}/\gamma-d}\rfloor,

where the constants
c_{r},c_{w},c_{m_{0}},c_{m}
will be specified later in the proof.

Similar to the proof of the case of
\gamma\beta\geq\beta_{P}
, we consider a packing
\{x_{k}\}_{k=1,\cdots,m}
with radius
2r_{P}
in
[0,1]^{d}
. Define
B_{c}
as the compliment of these
m
balls, i.e.,

B_{c}:=[0,1]^{d}/\left(\bigcup_{k=1}^{m}B(x_{k},2r_{P})\right).

Next, we further consider a packing
\{x_{k,l}\}_{l=1,\cdots,m_{0}}
for any
k=1,\cdots,m
with radius
2r_{Q}
. By scaling the radius of the balls, the feasibility of our considered packing reduces to finding a packing
\{x_{l}\}_{l=1,\cdots,m_{0}}
of
B(0,1)
with radius
r_{Q}/r_{P}
. We denote
r_{Q}/r_{P}
by
R
, and
m_{0}
then becomes
\lfloor c_{m_{0}}R^{-d}\rfloor
.

We provide an example of the reduced form of packing as follows. Consider the inscribed cube of
B(0,1)
with diagonal length
2
and side length
\frac{2}{\sqrt{d}}
. We divide this inscribed cube into uniform small cubes with side length as
6R
, which forms a grid with at least

\lfloor\frac{2}{\sqrt{d}}(6R)^{-1}\rfloor^{d}\geq\lfloor c\frac{2}{\sqrt{d}}(6R)^{-d}\rfloor

small cubes for some constant
c>0
. Therefore, provided that
c_{m_{0}}\leq\frac{2c}{6^{d}\sqrt{d}}
is small enough, we could assign exactly one sphere with radius
2R
in one cube without intersection, which forms a packing
\{x_{l}\}_{l=1,\cdots,m_{0}}
with radius
2R
. Hence, we can find a packing
\{x_{k,l}\}_{l=1,\cdots,m_{0}}
for any
k=1,\cdots,m
with radius
2r_{Q}
.

For any
\sigma\in\{1,-1\}^{m}
, we consider the regression functions
\eta^{Q}_{\sigma}(x)
and
\eta^{P}_{\sigma}(x)
defined as follows:

\eta^{Q}_{\sigma}(x)=\begin{cases}\frac{1}{2}\left(1+\sigma_{k}(C_{\beta}\land(\frac{C_{\beta_{P}}}{C_{\gamma}})^{\frac{1}{\gamma}})r_{Q}^{\beta}g^{\beta}(\frac{\|x-x_{k,l}\|}{r_{Q}})\right)\quad&\text{if $x\in B(x_{k,l},2r_{Q})$ for some $k,l$,}\\
\frac{1}{2}\quad&\text{otherwise,}\end{cases}

(87)

\eta^{P}_{\sigma}(x)=\begin{cases}\frac{1}{2}\left(1+\sigma_{k}C_{\beta_{P}}r_{P}^{\beta_{P}}g^{\beta_{P}}(\frac{\|x-x_{k}\|}{r_{P}})\right)\quad&\text{if $x\in B(x_{k},2r_{P})$ for some $k$,}\\
\frac{1}{2}\quad&\text{otherwise,}\end{cases}

(88)

where the function
g(\cdot)
is defined as
g(x)=\min\{1,2-x\}
on
x\in[0,2]
.

The construction of the marginal distributions
Q_{\sigma,X}
and
P_{\sigma,X}
is as follows. Let
Q_{\sigma,X}=P_{\sigma,X}
, both of which have the density function
\mu(\cdot)
defined as

\mu(x)=\begin{cases}\frac{w}{\lambda[B(x_{k},r_{Q})]}\quad&\text{if $x\in B(x_{k,l},r)$ for some $k,l$,}\\
\frac{1-mm_{0}w}{1-m\lambda[B(x_{k},2r_{P})]}\quad&\text{if $x\in B_{c}$,}\\
0\quad&\text{otherwise.}\end{cases}

Given the the construction of
(Q_{\sigma},P_{\sigma})
, we next verify that
(Q_{\sigma},P_{\sigma})
belongs to
\Pi^{NP}_{0}
for any
\sigma\in\{1,-1\}^{m}
.

Verify Margin Assumption:
We have that

\displaystyle Q_{\sigma}(0<|\eta_{\sigma}^{Q}-\frac{1}{2}|<t)

\displaystyle=mm_{0}Q_{\sigma}(0<(C_{\beta}\land(\frac{C_{\beta_{P}}}{C_{\gamma}})^{\frac{1}{\gamma}})r_{Q}^{\beta}g^{\beta}(\frac{\|X-x_{1,1}\|}{r_{Q}})\leq 2t)

\displaystyle=mm_{0}Q_{\sigma}(0<g(\frac{\|X-x_{1}\|}{r_{Q}})\leq(\frac{2t}{(C_{\beta}\land(\frac{C_{\beta_{P}}}{C_{\gamma}})^{\frac{1}{\gamma}})r_{Q}^{\beta}})^{\frac{1}{\beta}})

\displaystyle=mm_{0}w\mathbf{1}\{t\geq\frac{1}{2}(C_{\beta}\land(\frac{C_{\beta_{P}}}{C_{\gamma}})^{\frac{1}{\gamma}})r_{Q}^{\beta}\}

\displaystyle\leq c_{m}c_{m_{0}}c_{w}r_{P}^{\alpha\beta_{P}/\gamma}\mathbf{1}\{t\geq\frac{1}{2}(C_{\beta}\land(\frac{C_{\beta_{P}}}{C_{\gamma}})^{\frac{1}{\gamma}})r_{Q}^{\beta}\}

\displaystyle=c_{m}c_{m_{0}}c_{w}r_{Q}^{\alpha\beta}\mathbf{1}\{t\geq\frac{1}{2}(C_{\beta}\land(\frac{C_{\beta_{P}}}{C_{\gamma}})^{\frac{1}{\gamma}})r_{Q}^{\beta}\}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\alpha}t^{\alpha}

given that
c_{m}
is small enough. Therefore,
Q_{\sigma}\in\mathcal{M}(\alpha,C_{\alpha})
.

Verify Smoothness Assumption:

It is easy to see that for any
a,b\in[0,2]
we have

|g^{\beta}(a)-g^{\beta}(b)|\leq|a-b|^{\beta},\quad|g^{\beta_{P}}(a)-g^{\beta_{P}}(b)|\leq|a-b|^{\beta_{P}}.

Thus, for any
x,x^{\prime}\in B(x_{k,l},2r_{Q})
, we obtain from the triangular inequality
\|x-x_{k}\|-\|x^{\prime}-x_{k}\|\leq\|x-x^{\prime}\|
that

|r_{Q}^{\beta}g^{\beta}(\|x-x_{k,l}\|/r_{Q}))-r_{Q}^{\beta}g^{\beta}(\|x^{\prime}-x_{k,l}\|/r_{Q}))|\leq\|x-x^{\prime}\|^{\beta}.

(89)

By the definition of
\eta^{Q}_{\sigma}
and (
89
), we see that

|\eta^{Q}_{\sigma}(x)-\eta^{Q}_{\sigma}(x^{\prime})|\leq C_{\beta}\|x-x^{\prime}\|^{\beta},\quad\forall x,x\in[0,1]^{d}.

In addition, for any
x,x^{\prime}\in B(x_{k},2r_{P})
, we obtain from the triangular inequality
\|x-x_{k}\|-\|x^{\prime}-x_{k}\|\leq\|x-x^{\prime}\|
that

|r_{P}^{\beta_{P}}g^{\beta_{P}}(\|x-x_{k}\|/r_{P}))-r_{P}^{\beta_{P}}g^{\beta_{P}}(\|x^{\prime}-x_{k}\|/r_{P}))|\leq\|x-x^{\prime}\|^{\beta_{P}}.

(90)

By the definition of
\eta^{P}_{\sigma}
and (
90
), we see that

|\eta^{P}_{\sigma}(x)-\eta^{P}_{\sigma}(x^{\prime})|\leq C_{\beta_{P}}\|x-x^{\prime}\|^{\beta_{P}},\quad\forall x,x\in[0,1]^{d}.

Hence,
(Q,P)\in\mathcal{H}(\beta,\beta_{P},C_{\beta},C_{\beta_{P}})
.

Verify Strong Density Condition:

If
x\in B(x_{k,l},r)
for some
k=1,\cdots,m
and
l=1,\cdots,m_{0}
, we have

\mu(x)=\frac{w}{\lambda[B(x_{k,l},r)]}=c_{w}/\pi_{d}.

If
x\in B_{c}
, we have

\displaystyle\mu(x)=

\displaystyle\frac{1-c_{w}\lfloor c_{m_{0}}\frac{r_{P}^{d}}{r_{Q}^{d}}\rfloor\lfloor c_{m}r_{P}^{\alpha\beta_{P}/\gamma-d}\rfloor r_{Q}^{d}}{1-2^{d}\pi_{d}\lfloor c_{m}r_{P}^{\alpha\beta_{P}/\gamma-d}\rfloor r_{P}^{d}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1-c_{w}\lfloor c_{m_{0}}r_{Q}^{d\gamma\beta/\beta_{P}-d}\rfloor\lfloor c_{m}r_{Q}^{\alpha\beta-d\gamma\beta/\beta_{P}}\rfloor r_{Q}^{d}}{1-2^{d}\pi_{d}\lfloor c_{m}r_{P}^{\alpha\beta-d\gamma\beta/\beta_{P}}\rfloor r_{Q}^{d\gamma\beta/\beta_{P}}}\overset{n_{Q}\rightarrow\infty}{\longrightarrow}1.

Therefore, we could set
c_{w}\in[\pi_{d}\mu^{-},\pi_{d}\mu^{+}]
to satisfy the condition
(Q_{\sigma},P_{\sigma})\in\mathcal{S}(\mu^{+},\mu^{-},c_{\mu},r_{\mu})
.

Verify Signal Transfer Set:

We show that
\Omega^{+}(\gamma,C_{\gamma})=\Omega
for our construction of
(Q_{\sigma},P_{\sigma})
. By the construction of the marginal distribution pair
(Q^{\sigma}_{X},P^{\sigma}_{X})
and the definition of
\eta^{Q}_{\sigma}
and
\eta^{P}_{\sigma}
in (
87
) and (
88
), for any
x\in\Omega=B_{c}\cup\left(\bigcup_{k=1}^{m}B(x_{k,l},r_{Q})\right)
,

•

If
x\in B(x_{k,l},r)
for some
k=1,\cdots,m
and
l=1,\cdots,m_{0}
, we have

\eta^{Q}_{\sigma}(x)-\frac{1}{2}=\sigma_{k}(C_{\beta}\land(\frac{C_{\beta_{P}}}{C_{\gamma}})^{\frac{1}{\gamma}})r_{Q}^{\beta},\quad\eta^{P}_{\sigma}(x)-\frac{1}{2}=\sigma_{k}C_{\beta_{P}}r_{P}^{\beta_{P}}=\sigma_{k}C_{\beta_{P}}r_{Q}^{\gamma\beta}.

Therefore,

\mathrm{sgn}\left(\eta^{Q}(x)-\frac{1}{2}\right)\times(\eta^{P}(x)-\frac{1}{2})\geq C_{\gamma}|\eta^{Q}(x)-\frac{1}{2}|^{\gamma}.

•

If
x\in B_{c}
, we have
\eta^{Q}_{\sigma}(x)=\eta^{P}_{\sigma}(x)=\frac{1}{2}
. Therefore,

\mathrm{sgn}\left(\eta^{Q}(x)-\frac{1}{2}\right)\times(\eta^{P}(x)-\frac{1}{2})\geq C_{\gamma}|\eta^{Q}(x)-\frac{1}{2}|^{\gamma}.

Putting all verification steps above together, we conclude that
(Q_{\sigma},P_{\sigma})\in\Pi^{NP}_{0}
. We finish the proof of this part by applying Assouad’s lemma to
(Q_{\sigma},P_{\sigma}),\ \forall\sigma\in\{1,-1\}^{m}
.

If
\sigma,\sigma^{\prime}\in\{1,-1\}^{m}
differ only at one coordinate, i.e.

\sigma_{k}=-\sigma^{\prime}_{k},\quad\sigma_{l}=\sigma^{\prime}_{l}\ (\forall l\neq k),

we have the Hellinger distance bound as

\displaystyle H^{2}(Q_{\sigma},Q_{\sigma^{\prime}})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\int\left(\sqrt{\eta^{Q}_{\sigma}(X)}-\sqrt{\eta^{Q}_{\sigma^{\prime}}(X)}\right)^{2}+\left(\sqrt{1-\eta^{Q}_{\sigma}(X)}-\sqrt{1-\eta^{Q}_{\sigma^{\prime}}(X)}\right)^{2}dQ_{X}

\displaystyle=\sum_{l=1}^{m_{0}}\int_{B(x_{k,l},r_{Q})}\frac{w}{\lambda[B(x_{k,l},r_{Q})]}\left(\sqrt{\frac{1}{2}+(C_{\beta}\land(\frac{C_{\beta_{P}}}{C_{\gamma}})^{\frac{1}{\gamma}})r_{Q}^{\beta}}-\sqrt{\frac{1}{2}-(C_{\beta}\land(\frac{C_{\beta_{P}}}{C_{\gamma}})^{\frac{1}{\gamma}})r_{Q}^{\beta}}\right)^{2}dx

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}m_{0}w(1-\sqrt{1-2(C^{2}_{\beta}\land(\frac{C_{\beta_{P}}}{C_{\gamma}})^{\frac{2}{\gamma}})r_{Q}^{2\beta}})

\displaystyle\leq(C^{2}_{\beta}\land(\frac{C_{\beta_{P}}}{C_{\gamma}})^{\frac{2}{\gamma}})m_{0}wr_{Q}^{2\beta},

\displaystyle H^{2}(P_{\sigma},P_{\sigma^{\prime}})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\int\left(\sqrt{\eta^{P}_{\sigma}(X)}-\sqrt{\eta^{P}_{\sigma^{\prime}}(X)}\right)^{2}+\left(\sqrt{1-\eta^{P}_{\sigma}(X)}-\sqrt{1-\eta^{P}_{\sigma^{\prime}}(X)}\right)^{2}dP_{X}

\displaystyle=\sum_{l=1}^{m_{0}}\int_{B(x_{k,l},r_{Q})}\frac{w}{\lambda[B(x_{k,l},r_{Q})]}\left(\sqrt{\frac{1}{2}+C_{\beta_{P}}r_{P}^{\beta_{P}}}-\sqrt{\frac{1}{2}-C_{\beta_{P}}r_{P}^{\beta_{P}}}\right)^{2}dx

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}m_{0}w(1-\sqrt{1-2C^{2}_{\beta_{P}}r_{P}^{2\beta_{P}}})

\displaystyle\leq m_{0}wC^{2}_{\beta_{P}}r_{P}^{2\beta_{P}}.

By the property of Hellinger distance, we have

\displaystyle H^{2}(\mathbb{P}^{\sigma}_{\mathcal{D}_{Q}}\times\mathbb{P}^{\sigma}_{\mathcal{D}_{P}},\mathbb{P}^{\sigma^{\prime}}_{\mathcal{D}_{Q}}\times\mathbb{P}^{\sigma^{\prime}}_{\mathcal{D}_{P}})\leq

\displaystyle n_{Q}H^{2}(Q_{\sigma},Q_{\sigma^{\prime}})+n_{P}H^{2}(P_{\sigma},P_{\sigma^{\prime}})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(C^{2}_{\beta}\land(\frac{C_{\beta_{P}}}{C_{\gamma}})^{\frac{2}{\gamma}})m_{0}wn_{Q}r_{Q}^{2\beta}+m_{0}wC^{2}_{\beta_{P}}n_{P}r_{P}^{2\beta_{P}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle c_{w}c_{m_{0}}(C^{2}_{\beta}\land(\frac{C_{\beta_{P}}}{C_{\gamma}})^{\frac{2}{\gamma}})c_{r}^{2\beta}+c_{w}c_{m_{0}}C^{2}_{\beta_{P}}c_{r}^{2\gamma\beta}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\sqrt{2}}{4}

provided that
c_{r}
is small enough. This further indicates that

TV(\mathbb{P}^{\sigma}_{\mathcal{D}_{Q}}\times\mathbb{P}^{\sigma}_{\mathcal{D}_{P}},\mathbb{P}^{\sigma^{\prime}}_{\mathcal{D}_{Q}}\times\mathbb{P}^{\sigma^{\prime}}_{\mathcal{D}_{P}})\leq\sqrt{2}H^{2}(\mathbb{P}^{\sigma}_{\mathcal{D}_{Q}}\times\mathbb{P}^{\sigma}_{\mathcal{D}_{P}},\mathbb{P}^{\sigma^{\prime}}_{\mathcal{D}_{Q}}\times\mathbb{P}^{\sigma^{\prime}}_{\mathcal{D}_{P}})\leq\frac{1}{2}.

(91)

For any empirical classifier
\hat{f}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{Q_{\sigma}}(\hat{f})+\mathcal{E}_{Q_{\sigma^{\prime}}}(\hat{f})

\displaystyle=2\mathbb{E}_{Q_{\sigma}}[|\eta^{Q_{\sigma}}(X)-\frac{1}{2}|\mathbf{1}\{\hat{f}(X)\neq f^{*}_{Q_{\sigma}}(X)\}]

\displaystyle+2\mathbb{E}_{Q_{\sigma^{\prime}}}[|\eta^{Q_{\sigma^{\prime}}}(X)-\frac{1}{2}|\mathbf{1}\{\hat{f}(X)\neq f^{*}_{Q_{\sigma^{\prime}}}(X)\}]

\displaystyle\geq 2\sum_{l=1}^{m_{0}}\int_{B(x_{k,l},r_{Q})}\frac{w}{\lambda[B(x_{k,l},r_{Q})]}(C_{\beta}\land(\frac{C_{\beta_{P}}}{C_{\gamma}})^{\frac{1}{\gamma}})m_{0}wr_{Q}^{\beta}dx

\displaystyle=2(C_{\beta}\land(\frac{C_{\beta_{P}}}{C_{\gamma}})^{\frac{1}{\gamma}})m_{0}wr_{Q}^{\beta}.

Combining this lower bound with (
91
), the Assouad’s lemma shows that

\displaystyle\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}(Q,P)\in\Pi^{NP}_{0}\\
\Omega=\Omega_{P},\gamma\beta<\beta_{P}\end{subarray}}\mathbb{E}\mathcal{E}_{Q}(\hat{f})\geq\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}(Q_{\sigma},P_{\sigma})\\
\sigma\in\{1,-1\}^{m}\end{subarray}}\mathbb{E}\mathcal{E}_{Q_{\sigma}}(\hat{f})

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2}(C_{\beta}\land(\frac{C_{\beta_{P}}}{C_{\gamma}})^{\frac{1}{\gamma}})mm_{0}wr_{Q}^{\beta}

\displaystyle\gtrsim r_{Q}^{\beta(1+\alpha)}\gtrsim n_{P}^{-\frac{\beta_{P}(1+\alpha)/\gamma}{2\beta_{P}+d}}\land n_{Q}^{-\frac{\beta_{P}(1+\alpha)/\gamma}{2\beta_{P}/\gamma+d}}.

∎

E.4
Proof of Lemma
5
and
6

For any
i=1,\cdots,n_{Q}
, define the residual as
\varepsilon_{i}:=\psi^{\prime}(\beta_{Q}^{T}X_{i})-Y_{i}\in[-1,1]
. Define
V_{ij}:=X_{ij}\varepsilon_{i}
, where
X_{ij}
is the
j-
th covariate of the
i
-th observation
X_{i}
.
For any
t\in\mathbb{R}
, we compute the cumulant function

\displaystyle\log\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}_{Q}}[\exp(tV_{ij})|X_{i}]

\displaystyle=\log\left(\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}_{Q}}[\exp(t)X_{ij}Y_{i}]\exp(-tX_{ij}\psi^{\prime}(\beta_{Q}^{T}X_{i}))\right)

\displaystyle=\psi(tX_{ij}+\beta_{Q}^{T}X_{i})-\psi(\beta_{Q}^{T}X_{i})-\psi^{\prime}(\beta_{Q}^{T}X_{i})tX_{ij}.

Hence, by the second-order Taylor series expansion, we have

\log\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}_{Q}}[\exp(tV_{ij})|X_{i}]=\frac{t^{2}}{2}X_{ij}^{2}\psi^{\prime\prime}(\beta_{Q}^{T}X_{i}+\xi_{i}tX_{ij})\leq\frac{t^{2}}{8}(\frac{1}{n_{Q}}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{Q}}X_{ij}^{2}).

(92)

for some
\xi_{i}\in[0,1]
.
Since
\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}_{Q}}[X_{ij}^{2}]=1
and
X_{ij}^{2}
is sub-exponential since
X_{ij}\sim N(0,1)
, the tail bound for the independent sum of sub-exponential random variables reads

\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{D}_{Q}}(\frac{1}{n_{Q}}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{Q}}X_{ij}^{2}\geq 2)\leq 2\exp(-n_{Q}/4).

We denote the event
E:=\{\max_{j=1,\cdots,d}\frac{1}{n_{Q}}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{Q}}X_{ij}^{2}\leq 2\}
. The union bounds give

\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{D}_{Q}}(E^{c})\leq\sum_{j=1}^{d}\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{D}_{Q}}(\frac{1}{n_{Q}}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{Q}}X_{ij}^{2}\geq 2)\leq 2d\exp(-n_{Q}/4).

(93)

By (
92
), we have that on the event
E
,

\log\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}_{Q}}[\exp(tV_{ij})|X_{i}]\leq\frac{t^{2}}{4},

and we obtain by the Chernoff bound that

\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{D}_{Q}}(|\frac{1}{n_{Q}}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{Q}}V_{ij}|\geq t|E)\leq 2\exp(-4n_{Q}t^{2}),

and the union bound is as follows:

\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{D}_{Q}}(\max_{j=1,\cdots,d}|\frac{1}{n_{Q}}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{Q}}V_{ij}|\geq t|E)\leq 2d\exp(-4n_{Q}t^{2}).

(94)

We set
t=\frac{\sqrt{K+1}}{2}\sqrt{\frac{\log d}{n_{Q}}}
, then (
94
) becomes

\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{D}_{Q}}(\max_{j=1,\cdots,d}|\frac{1}{n_{Q}}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{Q}}V_{ij}|\geq\frac{\sqrt{K+1}}{2}\sqrt{\frac{\log d}{n_{Q}}}|E)\leq 2d^{-K}.

(95)

Putting (
93
) and (
95
) together, we have

\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{D}_{Q}}(\max_{j=1,\cdots,d}|\frac{1}{n_{Q}}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{Q}}V_{ij}|\geq\frac{\sqrt{K+1}}{2}\sqrt{\frac{\log d}{n_{Q}}})\leq 2d^{-K}+2d\exp(-n_{Q}/4).

(96)

Denote

E_{Q}:=\max_{j=1,\cdots,d}|\frac{1}{n_{Q}}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{Q}}V_{ij}|\leq\frac{\sqrt{K+1}}{2}\sqrt{\frac{\log d}{n_{Q}}},

then we have
\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{D}_{Q}}(E_{Q}^{c})\leq 2d^{-K}+2d\exp(-n_{Q}/4)
.

Define the empirical loss function as

l_{Q}(\beta):=\frac{1}{n_{Q}}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{Q}}\left\{\log(1+e^{X_{i}^{T}\beta})-Y_{i}X_{i}^{T}\beta\right\}.

From straightforward calculation,we have

\nabla l_{Q}(\beta)=\frac{1}{n_{Q}}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{Q}}(\psi^{\prime}(\beta^{T}X_{i})-Y_{i})X_{i}\Rightarrow\nabla l_{Q}(\beta_{Q})=\frac{1}{n_{Q}}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{Q}}V_{i},

\nabla^{2}l_{Q}(\beta)=\frac{1}{n_{Q}}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{Q}}\psi^{\prime\prime}(\beta^{T}X_{i})X_{i}X_{i}^{T}.

where
V_{i}=(\psi^{\prime}(\beta_{Q}^{T}X_{i})-Y_{i})X_{i}
. We see that
l_{Q}(\beta)
is convex since
\nabla^{2}l_{Q}(\beta)
is positive definite. In addition, we have

\|\nabla l_{Q}(\beta_{Q})\|_{\infty}=\max_{j=1,\cdots,d}|\frac{1}{n_{Q}}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{Q}}V_{ij}|.

Define the error of the first-order Taylor series expansion of
l_{Q}
at
\beta_{Q}
as

\delta l_{Q}(v)=l_{Q}(\beta_{Q}+v)-l_{Q}(\beta_{Q})-(\nabla l_{Q}(\beta_{Q}))^{T}v

for any
v\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
.

Define the support of
\beta_{Q}
as
S
with the cardinality
|S|\leq s
. Denote
\{1,\cdots,d\}/S
by
S^{c}
. Since we choose
\lambda_{Q}\geq\sqrt{K+1}\sqrt{\frac{\log d}{n_{Q}}}
, on the event
E_{Q}
we have
\lambda_{Q}\geq 2\|\nabla l_{Q}(\beta_{Q})\|_{\infty}
. Proposition 5.3 of
Fan et al., 2020
then implies that on the event
E_{Q}
, we have

\|(\hat{\beta}_{Q}-\beta_{Q})_{S^{c}}\|_{1}\leq 3\|(\hat{\beta}_{Q}-\beta_{Q})_{S}\|_{1}\Rightarrow\|\hat{\beta}_{Q}-\beta_{Q}\|\leq 4\|(\hat{\beta}_{Q}-\beta_{Q})_{S}\|_{1}.

Suppose that
n_{Q}
is large enough such that
n_{Q}\geq 64\kappa_{2}^{2}s\log d
, which is feasible as we assumed
n_{Q}\gg s\log d
. From Proposition 2 of the full version of
Negahban et al., 2009
, there exists constants
\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2},c_{1},c_{2}>0
such that for any
v\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
satisfying that
\|v\|_{2}\leq 1,\|v_{S^{c}}\|_{1}\leq 3\|v_{S}\|_{1}
, we have

\displaystyle\delta l_{Q}(v)

\displaystyle\geq\kappa_{1}\|v\|^{2}_{2}-\kappa_{1}\kappa_{2}\sqrt{\frac{\log d}{n_{Q}}}\|v\|_{1}\|v\|_{2}

(97)

\displaystyle\geq\kappa_{1}\|v\|_{2}(\|v\|_{2}-\frac{1}{8\sqrt{s}}\|v\|_{1})

\displaystyle\geq\kappa_{1}\|v\|_{2}(\|v\|_{2}-\frac{1}{2\sqrt{s}}\|v_{S}\|_{1})

\displaystyle\geq\kappa_{1}\|v\|_{2}(\|v\|_{2}-\frac{1}{2}\|v_{S}\|_{2})

\displaystyle\geq\kappa_{1}\|v\|_{2}(\|v\|_{2}-\frac{1}{2}\|v\|_{2})

\displaystyle=\frac{\kappa_{1}}{2}\|v\|_{2}^{2}

with probability at least
1-c_{1}\exp(-c_{2}n_{Q})
w.r.t. the distribution of
\mathcal{D}_{Q}
. See Appendix D.2 of the full version of
Negahban et al., 2009
to get a clearer view of this statement.

Putting (
96
) and (
97
) together, we obtain that with probability at least
1-2d^{-K}-2d\exp(-n_{Q}/4)-c_{1}\exp(-c_{2}n_{Q})
w.r.t. the distribution of
\mathcal{D}_{Q}
, we have

\delta l_{Q}(v)\geq\frac{\kappa_{1}}{2}\|v\|_{2}^{2}\quad\forall\|v\|_{2}\leq 1,\|v\|_{1}\leq 3\|v_{S}\|_{1},

(98)

and

\lambda_{Q}\geq 2\|\nabla l_{Q}(\beta_{Q})\|_{\infty}.

(99)

Applying Theorem 1 of the full version of
Negahban et al., 2009
with (
98
) and (
99
), we have that with probability at least
1-2d^{-K}-2d\exp(-n_{Q}/4)-c_{1}\exp(-c_{2}n_{Q})
,

\|\hat{\beta}_{Q}-\beta_{Q}\|\leq\frac{4\sqrt{s}}{\kappa_{1}}\lambda_{Q}=\frac{4c_{Q}}{\kappa_{1}}\sqrt{\frac{s\log d}{n_{Q}}}.

(100)

since
\|(\beta_{Q})_{S^{c}}\|_{1}=0
. Denote the event
\{\|\hat{\beta}_{Q}-\beta_{Q}\|^{2}\leq\frac{4\sqrt{s}}{\kappa_{1}}\lambda_{Q}\}
by
E_{0}
, we have

\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{D}_{Q}}(E_{0}^{c})\leq 2d^{-K}+2d\exp(-n_{Q}/4)+c_{1}\exp(-c_{2}n_{Q}).

By the theorem setting, we have
2d^{-K}\lesssim\frac{s\log d}{n_{Q}}\land\frac{s\log d}{n_{P}}
, so it remains to show that the term
2d\exp(-n_{Q}/4)+c_{1}\exp(-c_{2}n_{Q})
is asymptotically less than
\frac{s\log d}{n_{Q}}\land\frac{s\log d}{n_{P}}
. Given the condition
\log n_{Q}\gg d
, we could set
n_{Q}
to be large enough such that

2d\exp(-n_{Q}/4)=\exp(-n_{Q}/4+\log d)\leq\exp(-n_{Q}/8).

Hence, it suffices to show that for any
c>0
, we have
\exp(-cn_{Q})\ll\frac{s\log d}{n_{Q}}\land\frac{s\log d}{n_{P}}
.
Note that

n_{Q}\gg\log\frac{n_{P}}{s\log d}\Rightarrow\exp(cn_{Q}-\frac{n_{P}}{s\log d})\gg 1\Rightarrow\exp(-cn_{Q})\ll\frac{s\log d}{n_{P}}.

Since it is trivial that
\exp(-cn_{Q})\ll\frac{s\log d}{n_{Q}}
, we have
\exp(-cn_{Q})\ll\frac{s\log d}{n_{Q}}\land\frac{s\log d}{n_{P}}
, which finishes the proof.
∎

For any
i=1,\cdots,n_{P}
, define the residual as

\varepsilon^{P}_{i}:=\psi^{\prime}(\beta_{P}^{T}X^{P}_{i})-Y^{P}_{i}\in[-1,1].

Define
V^{P}_{ij}:=X^{P}_{ij}\varepsilon^{P}_{i}
, where
X^{P}_{ij}
is the
j-
th covariate of the
i
-th observation
X^{P}_{i}
. Following the completely identical procedures as given in Lemma
5
, we have that

\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{D}_{P}}(\max_{j=1,\cdots,d}|\frac{1}{n_{P}}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{P}}V^{P}_{ij}|\geq\frac{\sqrt{K+1}}{2}\sqrt{\frac{\log d}{n_{P}}})\leq 2d^{-K}+2d\exp(-n_{P}/4).

(101)

Denote

E_{P}:=\max_{j=1,\cdots,d}|\frac{1}{n_{Q}}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{P}}V_{ij}|\leq\frac{\sqrt{K+1}}{2}\sqrt{\frac{\log d}{n_{P}}},

then we have
\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{D}_{P}}(E_{P}^{c})\leq 2d^{-K}+2d\exp(-n_{P}/4)
. Similarly, define the empirical loss function as

l_{P}(\beta):=\frac{1}{n_{P}}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{P}}\left\{\log(1+e^{(X^{P}_{i})^{T}\beta})-Y^{P}_{i}(X^{P}_{i})^{T}\beta\right\}.

Similarly, we have

\|\nabla l_{P}(\beta_{Q})\|_{\infty}=\max_{j=1,\cdots,d}|\frac{1}{n_{P}}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{P}}V^{P}_{ij}|,

and

\nabla^{2}l_{P}(\beta)=\frac{1}{n_{P}}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{P}}\psi^{\prime\prime}(\beta^{T}X^{P}_{i})X^{P}_{i}(X^{P}_{i})^{T}.

Define

h=\frac{\|\beta_{P}\|}{\|\beta_{Q}\|}\beta_{Q},

which could be seen as the “rotated"
\beta_{P}
in order to have the sparsity pattern with the same norm. Due to the angle constraint with the parameter
\Delta
between
\beta_{Q}
and
\beta_{P}
, we have

\|\beta_{P}-h\|\leq\Delta\|\beta_{P}\|\leq U\Delta.

We expand the first-order Taylor series at
h
, which is different from the classical analysis in Lemma
5
. Define the error of the first-order Taylor series expansion of
l_{P}
at
h
as

\delta l_{P}(v)=l_{P}(h+v)-l_{P}(h)-(\nabla l_{P}(h))^{T}v

for any
v\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
. Define the support of
\beta_{Q}
as
S
. Since we choose
\lambda_{P}\geq\sqrt{K+1}\sqrt{\frac{\log d}{n_{P}}}
, on the event
E_{P}
we have
\lambda_{P}\geq 2\|\nabla l_{P}(\beta_{P})\|_{\infty}
.

Let
F(v)=l_{P}(h+v)-l(v)+\lambda_{P}(\|h+v\|_{1}-\|h\|_{1})
and
\hat{v}=\hat{\beta}_{P}-h
. Then
F(\hat{v})\leq 0
. It could be observed that on the event

E_{P}\cap\{\|\frac{1}{n_{P}}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{P}}X^{P}_{i}(X^{P}_{i})^{T}\|_{2}\leq 2\},

we have

\displaystyle l_{P}(h+v)-l(v)

\displaystyle\geq-|\nabla l_{P}(h)^{T}v|

(102)

\displaystyle\geq-|\nabla l_{P}(\beta_{P})^{T}v|-|(\nabla l_{P}(\beta_{P})-\nabla l_{P}(h))^{T}v|

\displaystyle\geq-\|\nabla l_{P}(\beta_{P})\|_{\infty}\|v\|_{1}-\|\nabla l_{P}(\beta_{P})-\nabla l_{P}(h)\|_{2}\|v\|_{2}

\displaystyle\geq-\frac{\lambda_{P}}{2}\|v\|_{1}-\max\{\|\nabla^{2}l_{P}\|_{2}\}\cdot\|\beta_{P}-h\|_{2}\cdot\|v\|_{2}

\displaystyle\geq-\frac{\lambda_{P}}{2}\|v\|_{1}-\frac{1}{4}\|\frac{1}{n_{P}}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{P}}X^{P}_{i}(X^{P}_{i})^{T}\|_{2}\cdot\|\beta_{P}-h\|_{2}\cdot\|v\|_{2}

\displaystyle\geq-\frac{\lambda_{P}}{2}\|v\|_{1}-\frac{U\Delta}{2}\|v\|_{2}.

The first inequality is due to convexity of
l_{p}
